# PaddleOCR-Rust Port Roadmap

Roadmap version: `0.1.0`  
Bootstrap date: `2026-08-02`  
Current phase: `P2–P4 — Contracts, model/runtime qualification, and geometry foundations`  
Current upstream reference: `2661c7c0ef5c613e8f93c6e93b2e052399f0f854`

## 1. Purpose and authority

This file is the canonical execution plan for PaddleOCR-Rust until the project
Definition of Finished is satisfied. `AGENTS.md` remains authoritative for
safety, repository boundaries, language policy, and engineering conduct. This
roadmap controls project scope, dependencies, decision gates, implementation
order, compatibility claims, milestones, and completion status.

Every repository change must map to a work-item ID in this roadmap. Before
starting work:

1. Read the relevant phase, dependencies, decisions, and acceptance criteria.
2. Set the item to `In progress` only when work actually begins.
3. Do not cross an unresolved decision gate or an incomplete dependency.
4. Implement the item together with its required tests, documentation,
   compatibility evidence, and security/licensing work.
5. Record validation evidence and mark it `Done` only after every acceptance
   criterion is satisfied.

If requested work is absent, conflicts with this plan, or changes its order,
scope, baseline, or Definition of Finished, amend this file before implementing
the request. Record the reason in the change log. A direct user instruction can
change the roadmap, but it does not silently bypass it.

Independent items may run in parallel only when their declared dependencies and
decision gates permit it. Later-phase work may start early under the same rule,
but no phase exit gate may be skipped. Nothing in this roadmap authorizes a
write through `./PaddleOCR`; that symlink and its target are always read-only.

## 2. Status and evidence rules

Work-item statuses are:

- `Planned`: scoped, but work has not begun.
- `In progress`: implementation, investigation needed for the deliverable, or
  validation has begun.
- `Blocked`: unfinished; the item must record the blocker, impact, and exact
  condition needed to resume.
- `Done`: all acceptance criteria are satisfied and validation evidence is
  recorded.
- `Deferred`: removed from the current milestone by a recorded user-approved
  scope decision; it is not complete.
- `Out of scope`: deliberately unsupported by a recorded user-approved scope
  decision; it is not complete and must not be advertised.

Compatibility rows use `Unassessed`, `Planned`, `In progress`, `Verified`,
`Intentional difference`, or `Out of scope`. Only a `Verified` capability may
be described as supported. A work item can be `Done` while recording an
`Intentional difference`, provided the difference is deliberate, documented,
tested, and approved by the relevant scope/API decision.

Evidence for every completed implementation item must identify:

- implementation paths;
- test and legally usable fixture paths;
- exact upstream commit/release and inspected source paths;
- relevant decision records;
- validation commands actually run and their results;
- numerical, geometry, text, ordering, and performance tolerances;
- known limitations and intentional differences.

Scaffolding, unvalidated code, an unrun required check, or a broad API with
placeholder behavior is never `Done`.

## 3. Fixed constraints

The finished implementation must remain:

- an independent native-Rust library and CLI, with no Python dependency at
  build time or runtime;
- independent of `./PaddleOCR` for builds, tests, packaging, installation, and
  runtime;
- backend-neutral at its public API boundary;
- explicit about exact model, language, platform, and schema compatibility;
- safe for untrusted images and documents within documented resource limits;
- reproducible, with model provenance and integrity verification;
- conservative about `unsafe`, bundled assets, native dependencies, and
  compatibility or benchmark claims;
- testable offline without a GPU or large model download for normal unit tests.

Python may be used only by separately documented maintainer-side oracle, model
conversion, or user-authorized external native-runtime build/ABI evidence
tooling in an isolated environment. The last category requires direct user
authorization and may use Python only as a native-runtime build or inspection
driver; it must not execute PaddleOCR or model inference. Such tooling must
never execute inside the linked upstream checkout, write through the symlink,
or become a consumer/build/runtime requirement.

## 4. Pinned upstream baseline

The initial behavioral inventory was inspected on 2026-08-02 from the local
read-only checkout:

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Repository link | `./PaddleOCR -> ../PaddleOCR` |
| Commit | `2661c7c0ef5c613e8f93c6e93b2e052399f0f854` |
| Commit date/context | 2026-07-22 checkout state |
| Upstream license | Apache License 2.0 for the repository source |
| Modern facade | `PaddleOCR/paddleocr/` |
| Classic implementation | `PaddleOCR/ppocr/`, `PaddleOCR/tools/` |
| Legacy structure implementation | `PaddleOCR/ppstructure/` |
| Native inference reference | `PaddleOCR/deploy/cpp_infer/` |
| Browser/ONNX reference | `PaddleOCR/paddleocr-js/packages/core/` |
| Tests and behavioral fixtures | `PaddleOCR/tests/` |

The modern `paddleocr/` package delegates substantial behavior to PaddleX
`>=3.7.0,<3.8.0`. `BASE-002` must pin the exact transitive PaddleX reference
needed to explain behavior before modern-pipeline parity is claimed. The
classic `ppocr/`, C++, and browser sources are useful independent references,
but none alone defines all modern behavior.

See [docs/PADDLEX_BASELINE.md](docs/PADDLEX_BASELINE.md) for the observed dependency range,
official candidate tags, delegated interfaces, and the explicit condition needed
to resolve the missing exact reference.

An upstream version change never silently changes this baseline. Updating it
requires an inventory diff, compatibility-ledger update, fixture/tolerance
review, and a roadmap amendment approved as a scope change.

## 5. Initial capability inventory

This inventory defines what must be classified during `INV-001` and `SCOPE-001`.
It does not claim Rust support.

The row-level baseline inventory is maintained in [docs/INVENTORY.md](docs/INVENTORY.md).
This section is the roadmap summary; `docs/INVENTORY.md` records the public facade,
classic algorithms/configurations, tests, assets, deployment, and ecosystem
surfaces that `SCOPE-001` must classify.

|---|---|---|---|
| Text detection | `paddleocr/_models/text_detection.py` | `PP-OCRv6_medium_det` | P5 |
| Text recognition | `paddleocr/_models/text_recognition.py` | `PP-OCRv6_medium_rec` | P5 |
| Text-line orientation | `paddleocr/_models/textline_orientation_classification.py` | `PP-LCNet_x0_25_textline_ori` | P5 |
| Document orientation | `paddleocr/_models/doc_img_orientation_classification.py` | `PP-LCNet_x1_0_doc_ori` | P7 |
| Text image unwarping | `paddleocr/_models/text_image_unwarping.py` | `UVDoc` | P7 |
| Layout detection | `paddleocr/_models/layout_detection.py` | `PP-DocLayout_plus-L` | P8 |
| Table classification | `paddleocr/_models/table_classification.py` | `PP-LCNet_x1_0_table_cls` | P8 |
| Table-cell detection | `paddleocr/_models/table_cells_detection.py` | `RT-DETR-L_wired_table_cell_det` | P8 |
| Table structure recognition | `paddleocr/_models/table_structure_recognition.py` | `SLANet` | P8 |
| Formula recognition | `paddleocr/_models/formula_recognition.py` | `PP-FormulaNet_plus-M` | P8 |
| Seal text detection | `paddleocr/_models/seal_text_detection.py` | `PP-OCRv4_mobile_seal_det` | P8 |
| Chart parsing | `paddleocr/_models/chart_parsing.py` | `PP-Chart2Table` | P8/P10 |

Expected result contracts include detection polygons/scores, recognized
text/scores, classification IDs/labels/scores, object boxes, table structure and
scores, unwarped images, formulas, and VLM structured results. Exact schemas are

### 5.3 Classic algorithm, training, and export surface

| Family | Baseline source | Included algorithms/capabilities | Planned track |
|---|---|---|---|
| Detection | `ppocr/modeling/heads/det_*.py`, `ppocr/postprocess/` | DB/DB++, EAST, SAST, PSE, FCE, CT, DRRG | P12 |
| End-to-end text spotting | `configs/e2e/`, PG heads/losses/postprocess | PGNet | P12 |
| Recognition | `ppocr/modeling/backbones/rec_*.py`, `heads/rec_*.py` | CTC/CRNN/PP-OCR/SVTR, SAR, SRN, NRTR, RARE, ABINet, VisionLAN, RobustScanner, SPIN, RFL, SATRN, ParseQ, CPPD | P12 |
| Formula recognition | recognition backbones/heads/losses | CAN, LaTeXOCR, UniMERNet, PP-FormulaNet | P8/P12 |
| Classification | `cls_head.py`, `cls_postprocess.py` | text/document orientation classifiers | P5/P7/P12 |
| Tables | `configs/table/`, table heads/postprocess | SLA/SLANet/TableMaster and HTML matching | P8/P9/P12 |
| KIE | `configs/kie/`, `ppstructure/kie/` | SDMGR, LayoutLM/LayoutLMv2/LayoutXLM SER/RE | P8/P12 |
| Super-resolution | `configs/sr/`, SR transforms/heads/losses | TSRN, TBSRN, Telescope | P8/P12 |
| Data and augmentation | `ppocr/data/` | simple, multiscale, LMDB, PGNet, PubTab, LaTeX datasets; label and augmentation registry | P12 |
| Training and evaluation | `tools/train.py`, `tools/eval.py`, `tools/program.py` | losses, metrics, optimizers, schedulers, AMP, distributed, EMA, checkpoints | P12 |
| Export and optimization | `tools/export_model.py`, `deploy/slim/` | export, distillation, pruning, quantization, compression | P12 |

The baseline contains roughly 155 training configurations. Algorithm-family
parity is therefore a separate late track, not part of the first OCR vertical
slice and not something a single inference milestone may claim.

### 5.4 Deployment and ecosystem surfaces

The inventory must classify the current C++ inference path, ONNX conversion,
Paddle Lite, Android/iOS demos, browser SDK, Docker/serving, cloud API SDKs,
high-performance/VLM serving, MCP server, and LangChain integration. A Rust port
may provide an equivalent interoperability surface instead of copying a
language-specific demo, but every omission or replacement needs an explicit
`SCOPE-001` classification. Translated upstream documentation and historical
release branches are provisional non-goals pending that decision.

## 6. Compatibility ledger requirements

`docs/COMPATIBILITY.md` will be created in P0 and maintained for the life of this
roadmap. Each row must contain:

| Field | Required meaning |
|---|---|
| Capability ID | Stable feature/work-item identifier |
| Priority | Must, Should, Later, or Out of scope |
| Upstream baseline | Exact commit/release |
| Upstream references | Public docs and source paths inspected |
| Observable contract | Inputs, defaults, outputs, ordering, and errors |
| Rust surface | Rust API, CLI, schema, or deployment surface |
| Model/artifact | Exact family, format, manifest identity, and checksum |
| Fixtures | Provenance-approved input and expected-result paths |
| Tolerances | Exact text, numeric, geometry, score, or performance rule |
| Status | Unassessed, Planned, In progress, Verified, Intentional difference, or Out of scope |
| Difference record | Decision record for each deliberate semantic difference |
| Evidence | Tests and validation runs supporting the row |

Compatibility is claimed per exact model artifact, backend, platform, and
output contract—not with an unqualified “PaddleOCR compatible” label.

## 7. Open decision register

No dependent implementation may assume an answer while a decision is open.

| ID | Decision | Required by | Resolution item | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `D-001` | Project/crate/package identity, license, notices, and trademark wording | Gate P0 | `DEC-001` | Resolved for bootstrap in `docs/P0_DECISIONS.md` |
| `D-002` | Full pinned-baseline completion scope and first-release subset | Gate P0 | `SCOPE-001`, `SCOPE-002` | Resolved for bootstrap in `docs/P0_DECISIONS.md` |
| `D-003` | Stable Rust policy/MSRV, OS/architecture matrix, CPU baseline | Gate P0 | `DEC-002` | Resolved for bootstrap in `docs/P0_DECISIONS.md` |
| `D-004` | Minimal workspace/crate boundaries and public API compatibility level | Gate P0/P1 | `DEC-002`, `FND-001` | Resolved for bootstrap in `docs/P0_DECISIONS.md` |
| `D-005` | First detector/recognizer artifacts and language/script scope | Gate P0 | `DEC-002` | Resolved for the M2 family; exact artifact ABI remains P3 |
| `D-006` | Inference/model format and backend, selected from measured proofs | Gate P3 | `RT-004` | Resolved on 2026-08-04 in `docs/ADR_RT004_RUNTIME_SELECTION.md`: ONNX Runtime 1.28.0 CPU through `ort` 2.0.0-rc.13 with dynamic loading, consuming the pinned ONNX export, with three named post-decision gates and a reversal condition |
| `D-007` | Model distribution, conversion, cache, offline, checksum, and licensing policy | Gate P3 | `DEC-003`, `MODEL-DEC-001` | M2 local-only policy resolved; exact artifact policy remains P3 |
| `D-008` | Image/PDF/office decoder choices and input resource limits | Gate P4/P7 | `IMG-DEC-001`, `DOCIO-DEC-001` | M2 image portion resolved on 2026-08-04 in `docs/IMAGE_DECODER_DECISION.md`: PNG-only via `png` 0.18.1, with JPEG deferred to `IMG-003`. The PDF/office portion is resolved on 2026-08-04 in `docs/ADR_DOCIO_DEC_001_PDF_AND_OFFICE.md`: office formats rejected permanently, PDF in scope and deferred behind a five-part entry gate |
| `D-009` | JSON schema/API parity level and intentional Python differences | Gate P2/P6 | `API-DEC-001` | Resolved for M2 in `docs/API_CONTRACT.md`; later pipeline schemas require their own contracts |
| `D-010` | Local VLM runtime versus server adapters and supported VLM families | Gate P10 | `VLM-DEC-001` | Open |
| `D-013` | Whether to pin PaddleX as a second read-only baseline, freeze P8 from captures alone, or declare P8 out of scope | Gate P8 | `docs/P8_BASELINE_FINDING.md`, `docs/PADDLEX_BASELINE.md` | **Resolved 2026-08-05 by user decision**: pin PaddleX `3.7.2` as a second read-only reference baseline. A range is not a baseline, so the exact version is recorded rather than inherited |
| `D-011` | Native Rust training framework/backend and Paddle checkpoint interoperability | Gate P12 | `TRAIN-DEC-001` | Open |
| `D-012` | Service, C ABI, WASM/browser, mobile, and accelerator release targets | Gate P11/P13 | `DEPLOY-DEC-001` | Open |

For the first OCR slice, `D-005` must compare at least:

- `PP-OCRv5_mobile`, the lowest-risk path because the upstream browser ONNX
  implementation already demonstrates a complete detector/recognizer slice;
- `PP-OCRv6_tiny` or `PP-OCRv6_small`, for a newer compact target; and
- `PP-OCRv6_medium`, the current Python and C++ default whose parity cost may be
  higher.

A compact v5/v6 model must not be described as parity with the current medium
default unless that exact claim is validated.

Delivery milestones are cumulative:

| Milestone | Outcome | Required phases/gates |
|---|---|---|
| `M0` Foundation | Standalone Cargo project, contracts, fixtures, CI, model/runtime decision | P0–P3 |
| `M1` First model | One chosen recognizer or detector runs safely on baseline CPU | P4–P5 component gate |
| `M2` Useful classic OCR | Detector → crop/sort → recognizer through Rust API and CLI | P6 |
| `M3` Robust documents | Orientation, unwarping, PDFs/multipage within defined scope | P7 |
| `M4` Structured documents | Layout/table/formula/seal and PP-Structure scope | P8–P9 |
| `M5` VLM and integration | Approved VLM, API/service, and deployment targets | P10–P11 |
| `M6` Training and algorithm breadth | Approved classic training/export/config families | P12 |
| `M7` Port completion | Pinned-baseline inventory reconciled and final release approved | P13–P14 |

Reaching `M2` is a useful Rust OCR release; it is not completion of the full
roadmap or proof of broad PaddleOCR parity.

Current phase status:

| Phase | Status | Exit condition |
|---|---|---|
| P0 | Done | Approved bootstrap baseline, scopes, decisions, ledger, budgets, and risks; exact model/runtime implementation remains later-gated. |
| P1 | Done | Standalone workspace and offline engineering gate passed in an isolated no-network validation. |
| P2 | In progress | Frozen classic/API contracts and oracle procedure are complete. Four legally reviewed, BGR-boundary classic ONNX fixtures now have exact artifact hashes and `m2-e2e-v1` records: one no-text result, one synthetic four-line reading-order result, one synthetic tall-crop rotation result, and one self-authored CJK Unicode result. A separate source-only mock capture now verifies the classic inclusive recognition score-filter boundary at `0.5`. Detector thresholds, malformed-input, resource-limit, decoder, tensor, and actual Rust differential evidence remain pending. |
| P3 | In progress | Artifact/ABI discovery and the backend qualification rubric are complete; user-authorized local ONNX candidates were hash- and graph-inspected, and source-level CTC blank/dictionary/space index construction is recorded for the recognizer. The external `tract-onnx` proof rejected the exact-artifact configuration on symbolic shape typing. A separately documented external `ort` dynamic-load route ran all six qualification shapes, bounded C API error/lifecycle probes, a short shared-session concurrency probe, a bounded Rust cancellation/recovery probe, and a read-only pre-adoption wrapper/FFI source review. A later temporary direct Python ONNX Runtime-to-Rust `ort` relay is bit-identical for all six LCG shapes under one common library, proving only one wrapper's raw wire path. The separate static-package terms supplement enabled one static/Paddle-versus-ONNX capture across all six shapes; every absolute error was below `1e-4`, but an unapproved near-zero relative-error denominator made the literal provisional relative metric fail and the required second fresh run was not performed. It is not an `m2-tensor-v1` pass or a backend decision. CPU-baseline portability, independent raw-tensor comparison, license/supply-chain review, long-soak, broader cancellation policy, adapter resource behavior, runtime dictionary semantics, and a backend decision remain pending. |
| P4 | In progress | Early checked geometry and private crop-pixel work are underway after `FND-002`; a narrow OpenCV crop component oracle exists, while bounded decoding, color semantics, tensors, broader OpenCV evidence, and the phase exit gate remain pending. |
| P5 | Planned | Verified detector, recognizer, and scoped orientation modules |
| P6 | Planned | End-to-end classic OCR API/CLI (`M2`) |
| P7 | Planned | Verified document preprocessing and multipage scope (`M3`) |
| P8 | Planned | Verified scoped structure/specialized modules |
| P9 | Planned | Verified structured document pipelines (`M4`) |
| P10 | Planned | Verified scoped VLM/GenAI capabilities |
| P11 | Planned | Verified service/deployment/ecosystem targets (`M5`) |
| P12 | Planned | Verified scoped training/config families (`M6`) |
| P13 | In progress | Final security, licensing, performance, and platform gate |
| P14 | Planned | Reconciled baseline and user-approved release (`M7`) |

## 9. Global capability Definition of Done

A supported capability is complete only when:

1. Its Rust contract documents inputs, outputs, defaults, units, coordinate
   convention, ordering, errors, concurrency, and resource limits.
2. Its exact upstream baseline and inspected behavior paths are recorded.
3. Preprocessing, model contract, inference, postprocessing, inverse geometry,
   and orchestration are implemented as applicable.
4. Legally usable fixtures cover success, malformed input, boundary conditions,
   empty output, representative non-ASCII input, and deterministic ordering.
5. Golden/differential tolerances are chosen before final comparison, justified,
   and pass.
6. API, CLI, schema, model, language, platform, and known differences are
   documented without broader claims.
7. Normal tests require no network, Python, GPU, large model download, or
   upstream checkout.
8. Security, licensing, memory, and performance consequences are reviewed.
9. The compatibility-ledger row is `Verified` or records an approved, tested
   `Intentional difference`.
10. All required validation commands were actually run and recorded.

## 10. Phase plan

### P0 — Governance, baseline, and product scope

| ID | Status | Dependencies | Deliverable and acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GOV-001` | Done | None | `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and this roadmap define language, read-only upstream, roadmap authority, statuses, gates, and completion rules consistently. |
| `BASE-001` | Done | None | Record upstream commit, inspection date, license, and primary source surfaces without modifying upstream. Evidence is in Sections 4–5. |
| `BASE-002` | Done | `BASE-001` | M2 explicitly defers modern PaddleX-wrapper/pipeline parity; see `docs/PADDLEX_BASELINE.md` and `docs/P0_DECISIONS.md`. An exact PaddleX oracle remains required before modern-parity work or claims. Do not add PaddleX as a Rust runtime dependency. |
| `INV-001` | Done | `BASE-001` | `docs/INVENTORY.md` is the row-complete inventory of public modules, pipelines, classic algorithms/configs, outputs, CLI/API, deployment, assets, and tests. Inventory never implies support. |
| `SCOPE-001` | Done | `INV-001` | `docs/SCOPE_PROPOSAL.md`, `docs/SCOPE_CLASSIFICATION.md`, and `docs/P0_DECISIONS.md` preserve the Full Port Target and classify all inventory work without an M7 exclusion. |
| `SCOPE-002` | Done | `SCOPE-001` | The complete pinned-baseline target is frozen for M7. Every current non-M2 row is `Later`, not excluded; any future exclusion or Rust-equivalent replacement needs explicit user approval. |
| `DEC-001` | Done | `BASE-001` | `docs/P0_DECISIONS.md`, `LICENSE`, and `NOTICE` resolve identity, Apache-2.0 license, attribution, and independent-branding direction. Asset-specific reviews remain later work. |
| `DEC-002` | Done | `SCOPE-001` | `docs/P0_DECISIONS.md` resolves M2 scope, Linux x86-64/Rust 1.94 policy, one-package API direction, and the v6 medium model-family target. |
| `DEC-003` | Done | `DEC-001`, `DEC-002` | M2 accepts only explicitly provisioned local models; no automatic acquisition, cache, conversion, or bundled weights. P3 still resolves artifact-specific `D-007`. |
| `QUAL-001` | Done | `DEC-002` | `docs/QUALITY_PROFILE.md` records reference hardware plus predeclared correctness, determinism, latency, memory, binary-size, and resource budgets. |
| `COMP-001` | Done | `SCOPE-001` | `docs/COMPATIBILITY.md` has the complete M2 Must ledger with upstream paths, intended surfaces, fixture plans, and comparison measures. |
| `RISK-001` | Done | All P0 decisions | `docs/RISK_REGISTER.md` covers licenses, operator gaps, conversion drift, Unicode, geometry, hostile inputs, downloads/assets, native code, platforms, and performance. |

Gate P0 requires an approved scope, baseline, identity/license direction,
platform/toolchain targets, first model slice, initial API/package direction,
quality budgets, compatibility ledger, and risk register. No runtime or crate
architecture may be selected by preference alone.

Gate P0 passed for the approved M2 bootstrap on 2026-08-02. This does not
select an inference backend or exact model artifact; those remain P3 gates.

### P1 — Rust workspace and engineering foundation

| ID | Status | Dependencies | Deliverable and acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| `FND-001` | Done | Gate P0 | Created the single-package Rust 2024 workspace, pinned Rust 1.94 policy, Apache-2.0 metadata, versioned `Cargo.lock`, minimal features, and an explicitly non-functional CLI. |
| `FND-002` | Done | `FND-001` | Implemented checked encoded-byte input, dimensions, finite points, quadrilaterals/polygons, scores, affine transforms, recognized text, page indices, and model identity in `src/types.rs`. Image decoding remains P4 work. |
| `FND-003` | Done | `FND-001` | Implemented structured input, resource, model, backend, I/O, unsupported, and cancellation errors in `src/error.rs`; foundation constructors validate user input without panicking. |
| `FND-004` | Done | `FND-002`, `FND-003` | `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` records only the implemented public `error`/`types` boundaries and defers private backend/image/pipeline boundaries until their responsibilities have code. |
| `FND-005` | Done | `FND-001` | Configured formatting, manifest lints, missing-docs/no-unsafe policy, release stripping, locked dependency policy, pinned toolchain, and local no-warning validation. |
| `DOCS-001` | Done | `GOV-001`, direct user request 2026-08-02 | Moved the supporting contribution guide into `docs/` per the user's repository-layout preference. The root retains only discovery/authority entrypoints (`README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, and this roadmap); local links and Markdown targets were updated and validated with the normal workspace gate. This is an organization change only; it does not alter capability scope, artifact policy, or compatibility status. |

Gate P1 requires a clean standalone clone to pass the normal quality gate and
exercise core types/errors/fixture infrastructure without models, network,
Python, GPU, or the upstream checkout. Once Cargo exists, the baseline is:

```sh
cargo fmt --all --check
cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings
cargo test --workspace --all-features
```

Gate P1 local acceptance passed on 2026-08-02 in an ephemeral `bwrap` sandbox:
only the Rust workspace files were copied into an isolated writable `/tmp`,
the root source was read-only, network was unshared, `PaddleOCR` was absent,
and formatting, Clippy, tests, documentation, and the explicitly unsupported
CLI behavior all passed. The later remote GitHub Actions evidence closed
`CI-001`; see its status row and the change log below.

Gate P2 requires evidence-ready contracts and legal offline fixtures before
behavioral implementation claims begin.

### P3 — Model contracts, runtime qualification, and model lifecycle

Artifact provenance/ABI discovery is intentionally allowed to overlap the
remaining fixture work once `CTR-001` and `CTR-002` are frozen. `FIX-001`
cannot produce a model-backed offline golden without an exact legal artifact,
so making `MOD-001` wait for the entire P2 gate would create a dependency
cycle. This early `MOD-001` work may identify and inspect local artifacts only;
it does not select a runtime, permit behavioral implementation, or justify a
compatibility claim. Runtime qualification and model-backed implementation
remain gated by the completed P2 evidence requirements.

The user-authorized external ONNX pair has passed exact file-hash and parse-only graph inspection; the recognizer now also has source-level CTC blank/ordered-dictionary/appended-space index-construction and aggregate Unicode-structure records in `docs/LOCAL_ONNX_CANDIDATE_INSPECTION.md`. It also records the structural static-versus-ONNX comparison: each ONNX package ships a byte-identical copy of the corresponding static program and eleven operator families have equal counts under an assumed decomposition mapping, but no ONNX node was traced to a static operation and the graphs are not node-for-node identical (the detector's four `1.batch_norm_` operations have no ONNX counterpart while the recognizer's three do, shape-manipulation counts diverge sharply such as 117 `1.reshape` against zero ONNX `Reshape`, composite operators decompose differently, and elementwise counts diverge). Decisions: consume published ONNX exports and perform no conversion, so any divergence from upstream has one possible cause rather than two; distribute zero model bytes, which keeps the licensing question with the party that chose the model; no downloads, cache, search path, or environment variable, so a command line plus the named files fully determines the output — which is also what makes the digest check meaningful, since there is nothing else the run could have loaded; SHA-256 integrity that is optional and whose absence is recorded rather than defaulted; provenance as repository, revision, digest, and byte count together, because a version name is not provenance; and separate licensing preserved structurally by not bundling. The syntax is `key = value` with dotted keys, parsed in about fifty lines, for the same reason the result writer is hand-rolled: adding a serialisation dependency for a thirty-field file trades a well-specified parser for supply-chain surface. The parser is strict where leniency would be dangerous — an unknown key, a duplicate key, an uppercase digest, a zero byte count, and a wrong schema version are all errors, because the failure mode of a lenient manifest parser is `detector.sha265` leaving an artifact unverified while the document looks complete. The class count is derived from the dictionary fields rather than stored, so a manifest cannot state a count that disagrees with itself. The committed fixture holds the pinned pair; twelve tests cover it and every rejection. The CLI's `--manifest` applies the manifest digests as the expected ones when the caller gave none, refuses a manifest whose entry count disagrees with the loaded dictionary, and passes identity into the result document. Path resolution stays with `MOD-003`; a URL here is provenance and is never fetched. |

Size is checked before digest so a wrong build is not a gigabyte read, and `..` in a model directory is refused. Mismatch detail lives in a returned type rather than in `Error`, which by policy carries no caller paths. |

Gate P3 requires every first-slice model component to run on the baseline CPU
from Rust with validated raw outputs and no Python/upstream runtime dependency.

### P4 — Bounded image, geometry, crop, and tensor foundations

`GEO-001` may begin before the complete P2/P3 gates because its declared
dependency is `FND-002` and it operates only on checked dimensions. This early
pure-geometry work cannot select a decoder/backend or make a behavioral OCR
claim; all decode, tensor, crop, and model-dependent work retains its listed
dependencies.

`CROP-001` and `GEO-002` may begin once the specific `GEO-001` crop-plan or
geometry sub-deliverable they consume has code and tests. Their remaining
decoder, pixel-oracle, and model-facing work stays in those owning items.
Contour extraction, exact OpenCV rectangle semantics, polygon offsetting, and
DB detector output are `DET-003` work, not a condition for completing this
early pure-geometry item. Such parallel P4 work is limited to private checked
buffers and geometry validation; it does not select an image decoder,
establish color semantics, or permit an OCR compatibility claim before the
remaining gates.

`GEO-001` may also provide a bounded private minimum-area quadrilateral
candidate over already checked polygon vertices. That narrow mathematical slice
does not establish OpenCV `minAreaRect` equivalence, contour behavior, DB
postprocessing, or detector support; those remain gated `DET-003` work.

`DB-001` may begin as a private, bounded raw-map kernel after `CTR-001` because
strict DB segmentation does not select a runtime or model artifact. It may
validate one borrowed finite map and produce the fixed M2 `value > 0.3` binary
mask only. Tensor rank/batch ABI, contour extraction, scoring, polygon offset,
inverse scaling, inference integration, and any detector claim remain later
P3–P5 work.

`DB-002` is a private, bounded connected-component scan over that already-owned
binary bitmap. It exposes only deterministic row-major-seed 8-connected
component bounds and pixel counts under explicit component/frontier limits. It
is a memory-safe precursor for later detector work, not an equivalent of OpenCV
`findContours`: it must not claim
`RETR_LIST`, holes, hierarchy, `CHAIN_APPROX_SIMPLE`, contour order, minimum
area rectangles, scoring, offsets, rescaling, or emitted detector boxes.

`CTC-001` may begin as a private bounded greedy-index kernel after `CTR-001`.
It may validate one borrowed finite `[time, classes]` score matrix, preserve
lowest-index ties, collapse adjacent raw class indexes before removing blank
`0`, and compute the selected-max mean or empty `0.0` score. It must not choose
a dictionary, assume logits/probabilities or softmax, validate a batched runtime
ABI, emit text, reverse Arabic, or claim recognizer support; those remain P3–P5
work.

The same carve-out covers the private `INTER_LINEAR` pixel resize in
`src/resize.rs`, because both classic preprocessing paths reach
`cv2.resize(..., INTER_LINEAR)` and its byte-level result is therefore part of
the observable contract. That module resizes only: it selects no colour
convention, no interpolation policy, and no target size. Which size the
detector or recognizer requests remains `DET-001` and `REC-001` work.

`TEN-001` may begin as a private, bounded normalization/layout kernel over an
already decoded and already sized interleaved image, ahead of the full
`IMG-002` and `MOD-001` gates. This carve-out was recorded on 2026-08-04 before
the corresponding implementation. Its permitted scope is arithmetic only: the
exact `float32` evaluation order of the two frozen classic normalizations, the
HWC-to-CHW layout change, zero right-padding, batch stacking, contiguity, and
checked allocation. It must not resize pixels, choose a model, select a
resize/interpolation implementation, validate a runtime tensor ABI, or claim
that its output equals a captured model input tensor; matching a captured
tensor within declared tolerances remains `PRE-001`, and the recognition
shape/batching policy remains `REC-001`. Because it only rearranges and scales
values that a caller already supplied, it cannot establish preprocessing
compatibility on its own.

Pre-gate research and self-authored input fixtures may record factual `D-008`
evidence in [`docs/IMAGE_DECODER_EVIDENCE.md`](docs/IMAGE_DECODER_EVIDENCE.md)
only when they add no Cargo dependency, decoder code, input behaviour, or
decision. Such work may make `IMG-DEC-001` `In progress`, but does not satisfy
Gate P2 or authorize an implementation before its declared dependencies.

| ID | Status | Dependencies | Deliverable and acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| `IMG-DEC-001` | Done | Gate P2 and `MOD-001` to close; pre-gate evidence only is permitted | Resolve the image-decoder and image-limit portion of `D-008`, including formats, metadata/orientation, color/alpha, native dependencies, licenses, and hard limits. `docs/IMAGE_DECODER_EVIDENCE.md` contains isolated `image` 0.25.10/OpenCV color-orientation observations, bounded no-AVX/no-FMA QEMU execution, a self-authored 1,438-attempt mutation corpus, full committed-input-oracle/supply-chain replay for both the minimal `image` 0.25.10 and direct `jpeg-decoder` 0.3.2 + `png` 0.18.1 routes, a direct `zune-jpeg` 0.5.15 BGR/strict/unsafe-toggle control, and exact finite JPEG and hybrid JPEG+PNG controls for `libjpeg-turbo-rs` 0.8.0 plus `png` 0.18.1. The hybrid record now includes 1,440 bounded JPEG and 720 bounded PNG mutations with zero caught panics plus one clean 256 MiB QEMU-TCG `qemu64`/no-NIC run whose guest flags omit AVX, AVX2, and FMA. Its 32,000,000-pixel RGB resource control first exposed a 189,584–189,652 KiB peak and SIGABRT under 180,000 KiB because separate 128 MiB output/BGR checks permitted two large buffers. A subsequent external single-buffer/fallible-output revision completed at 95,756 KiB under that same limit and returned a typed resource error at 80,000 KiB; it avoids that observed two-buffer abort, but it does not prove a checked total allocation envelope or bound codec-internal allocations. The first three pure-Rust variants retain a maximum JPEG component delta of 36 in that corpus; the hybrid control matches all fourteen 8-bit JPEG/PNG BGR controls and five negatives but has unchecked/raw-pointer/SIMD review scope and deliberately leaves 16-bit PNG policy unresolved. None is selected or ranked. A libjpeg-turbo command-level control is also exact for the ten committed JPEGs but lacks a runnable Rust binding, native boundary, or deployment proof. ### P5 — Classic OCR single-model modules

Detection and recognition may proceed in parallel after Gates P3–P4.

Corrected: the aspect-sorted order is split into consecutive batches of six, each with its own `max_wh_ratio` and padded width, and `recognize` runs the backend once per batch. |

Single-crop and multi-batch paths are the same code path; a class-count mismatch, an output row count that disagrees with its batch, and a crop count above the work-unit budget are all typed errors. |

Decoding concatenates retained classes in timestep order with **no** normalization, case folding, trimming, or whitespace collapsing, and a test pins that a configured `U+3000` entry stays distinct from the appended `U+0020`, because merging them would destroy a class the model separates. The dictionary accepts an already parsed entry list, so the caller owns file access and no dictionary content is committed here; a self-authored 18,708-entry placeholder reproduces the recorded `18,710` class arithmetic. Remaining: the recognizer batching path that feeds it, confidence validation against a captured result, and `REC-004` coverage. |

It is bounded by a 4,096-contour and 1,000,000-point limit with typed resource errors. Three behaviours that three earlier prototypes got wrong are now pinned by tests and comments: the border marker is a single constant rather than a per-contour counter, so the raster scan's `p == 1` test is the retrace guard; the right flag comes from the swept direction range, not an east-neighbour test, which is what leaves a one-pixel-thick ring discoverable as a hole; and contours are returned in reverse discovery order. Remaining for this item: minimum-area geometry, unclipping, fast and slow scoring, filtering, and rescale/round/clip. The upstream specification for those steps has been read verbatim and is recorded here so the next implementation does not re-derive it. `get_mini_boxes` calls `cv2.minAreaRect(contour)`, then `cv2.boxPoints`, sorts the four corners by `x`, and reindexes with the frozen rule `index_1/index_4` from comparing `points[1][1] > points[0][1]` and `index_2/index_3` from `points[3][1] > points[2][1]`; `sside` is `min(bounding_box[1])`. `unclip` builds a Shapely `Polygon(box)`, computes `distance = poly.area * unclip_ratio / poly.length`, then offsets with `pyclipper.PyclipperOffset()` using `AddPath(box, JT_ROUND, ET_CLOSEDPOLYGON)` and `Execute(distance)`. `box_score_fast` clips `floor(min)` and `ceil(max)` of each axis into `[0, w-1]` and `[0, h-1]`, allocates a `(ymax-ymin+1, xmax-xmin+1)` mask, shifts the box by `-xmin`/`-ymin`, fills it with `cv2.fillPoly(..., 1)`, and returns `cv2.mean(bitmap[ymin:ymax+1, xmin:xmax+1], mask)[0]`. `tools/capture_min_area_box_oracle.py` already records sixteen self-authored OpenCV `minAreaRect`/`boxPoints` cases covering axis-aligned, diamond, slanted, thin, degenerate one-point and two-point, collinear, concave, and duplicate-vertex inputs; Remaining: `DET-002` inference integration ahead of it, the slow score mode, and `DET-004` coverage. |

### P6 — End-to-end classic OCR, Rust API, schema, and CLI

| ID | Status | Dependencies | Deliverable and acceptance criteria |

| `OCR-001` | Done | Gate P5, `CROP-001` | `src/pipeline.rs` implements the full sequence: detect, stable reading-order sort, perspective crop, tall-crop counter-clockwise rotation, aspect-sorted recognition batching, original-order restore, and score filter. Verified end to end against four committed fixtures through real `PP-OCRv6_medium` artifacts (gate `G1`), including the tall-crop rotation and a Unicode line. Orientation classification is not implemented; the item lists it as optional and it is a separate model outside the selected M2 slice, which `docs/COMPATIBILITY.md` records as unsupported. |
| `OCR-002` | Done | `OCR-001`, `CTR-001` | Reading order is the upstream two-stage sort — a primary top-to-bottom then left-to-right ordering, followed by backwards same-row swaps under a ten-pixel row tolerance — and is pinned by tests for the backwards swap, for the exact ten-pixel boundary not merging rows, and for equal top-left corners preserving input order, which is the duplicate case. Coordinates are preserved through the documented inverse transform rather than recomputed. Batch-order restoration is tested within one batch and across three batches where batch order is the exact reverse of caller order. |
| `OCR-003` | Done | `OCR-001` | Bounded batch sizes, session reuse, threads, cancellation and time policy, memory limits, and whole-input versus per-item failure semantics are all now defined and implemented. Resource half: `docs/G3_RESOURCE_EVIDENCE.md` records the first measurement of every budget `docs/QUALITY_PROFILE.md` declared before implementation — cold CLI `4.23 s`/`15 s`, warm median `2.840 s`/`5 s`, warm p95 `2.923 s`/`10 s`, peak resident `464.3 MiB`/`2 GiB`, stripped binary `812,144` bytes/`100 MiB`, model artifacts in package `0` bytes, three cold runs byte-identical, `99%` of one CPU. Batch bounds come from `RunBudget` and `MAX_CROPS` = `1,000`; both sessions are created once and reused; intra-op and inter-op threads are `1`. Policy half: `src/control.rs` adds `RunControl` with a wall-clock budget and an `Arc<AtomicBool>` cancellation flag, surfaced as `OcrOptions::control` and `--time-budget-ms`. The policy is stated with its limit rather than implied: **a run is abandoned only at a stage boundary**, because a backend call cannot be interrupted without leaving the session undefined, so overshoot is bounded by one detector run or one recognition batch of six rather than being immediate. Demonstrated end to end — a `1 ms` budget on a real page returns `time budget exhausted before crop`, having completed the detector it could not interrupt. Cancellation is reported ahead of a timeout because it says more about why the run stopped. Failure semantics: **whole-input**, recorded in `run_classic_ocr`'s own documentation with its reason — the result document has no field marking a result as incomplete, so four lines returned from a nine-line page would be indistinguishable from a four-line page, and per-item recovery would have to be visible in the output type to be safe, which is an `API-001` decision this item does not preempt. |
| `LANG-001` | Done | `REC-004`, `SCOPE-001` | `docs/LANGUAGE_SUPPORT.md` records exactly one verified mapping — the pinned `PP-OCRv6_medium` pair with `ppocrv6_dict.txt`, all three digests named — and states its verified extent narrowly: ASCII Latin words plus the two CJK scalars `U+4F60 U+597D`, which is what the fixtures and one real page actually demonstrate. Against that it sets the measured census of what the dictionary *contains*, produced by the new `dictionary_census` example over `src/script.rs`: `15,565` CJK Unified scalars, `928` symbols, `672` emoji, `180` kana, `76` Greek, and so on to `18,708`. The gap is the point of the item. Nine percent of the dictionary is emoji and symbols; if a scalar count implied support this port would claim to read emoji from a photograph. The census names Unicode blocks and never languages, because `CJK Unified` is a block that Chinese, Japanese, and Korean all draw from and no count decides which an artifact was trained for. Also recorded as unsupported: language detection, automatic dictionary selection, script-specific normalization, and right-to-left or vertical layout, the last because the reading-order sort is top-to-bottom then left-to-right and would be wrong for Arabic, Hebrew, or vertical CJK. Adding a language requires a committed fixture reproduced by gate `G1`, not a larger dictionary. |
| `API-001` | Done | `OCR-001`-`OCR-003`, `D-009` | `src/api.rs` exposes a typed surface - `Artifacts`, `Dictionary`, `OcrOptions`, `TextLine`, `OcrEngine`, `recognize_png` - that leaks no backend or private type, takes every artifact as an explicit caller-supplied path, and enforces model identity before a session is created. Errors are the typed `Error` enum with no panics on any tested path, and `OcrOptions::control` carries the `OCR-003` cancellation and time policy. `OcrEngine::load` is the reusable session handle: loading measured `0.534 s`, paid once instead of per image, and an ignored gate verifies that a reused engine returns results identical to reloading for three pages and that repeating one page through the same engine agrees with itself, which is what "no state carries between images" means concretely. The concurrency position is recorded **and enforced by the type system**: the backend holds its session in a `RefCell`, so `OcrEngine` is `!Sync` and will not compile shared across threads, and a test asserts that property so a future lock cannot silently convert a compile error into a hidden queue. One engine per thread is the documented answer. |
| `SCHEMA-001` | Done | `API-001`, `CTR-002` | `src/result_json.rs` implements `paddleocr-rust/ocr-result/v1`: schema version, input identifier, input dimensions, model identity, per-line quadrilateral, exact text, and confidence. Serialisation is byte-deterministic by construction rather than by convention — the writer is hand-rolled, so there is no map-ordering or float-formatting behaviour to depend on — and three cold CLI runs over the same input produced byte-identical documents. Text is JSON-escaped but never otherwise transformed. JSONL is delivered: several images emit one document per line, each carrying its own `input.id`, so position is not the only thing identifying a result. The `model` block is filled from a `MOD-002` manifest and carries identity only — family, version, format, backend, and the three digests — not URLs, which belong with whoever provisions rather than in every document produced, and not byte counts, which say nothing a digest does not. It is `null` rather than absent without a manifest, because a field that appears and disappears would make the schema two schemas and a consumer could not tell "no manifest was given" from "this producer predates the field". `page_index` remains `null` by design until multipage input exists in P7; that is the field's documented value for single-image input, not a gap. |
| `CLI-001` | Done | `API-001`, `SCHEMA-001` | `src/main.rs` takes every artifact as an explicit path — runtime library, detector, recognizer, dictionary, image — with optional `--detector-sha256`/`--recognizer-sha256` identity checks, `--time-budget-ms`, and `--json`. Results go to stdout and diagnostics to stderr, so `score<TAB>text` and the JSON document are both pipeable. Nothing is downloaded, cached, or read from an environment variable, and a build without the `onnxruntime` feature says so and exits rather than pretending to work. Exit codes and the full invocation are documented in `README.md`. |
| `E2E-001` | Done | All above | `tests/end_to_end.rs` tests the public surface only, which is the point: unit tests reach inside the crate and can pass while the API a caller actually has is unusable. Seven offline cases need no artifacts, no network, and not even the `onnxruntime` feature — committed PNG dimensions, five corrupt or truncated inputs, a valid header with unusable pixel data, all three declared size limits rejected from the header before any pixel is read, dictionary failures, result-document version and determinism, and the frozen default thresholds. Five provisioned cases cover what only a real model can: no text yielding an empty result rather than a fabricated line, four lines in stable reading order, a rotated tall crop, a non-Latin script with exact scalars, mixed scripts on one page, three repeat runs identical, `drop_score` and `box_threshold` boundaries changing what survives, a missing artifact, a digest mismatch, a text file loaded as a model, four threads each with their own engine agreeing on the same page, and an engine remaining usable after three rejected inputs. One case had to be rewritten to record what is true rather than what would be nice: **swapping the detector and recognizer loads successfully**. Both declare the same tensor names and leave the constrained axes dynamic, so nothing in the declared ABI distinguishes them and load-time validation can only check what a model declares; the swap fails on first use as a typed error, and a declared digest refuses it before the runtime sees a byte. That is now the concrete argument for the digest arguments, recorded in `README.md`. |
| `DOC-USER-001` | Done | `E2E-001` | `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` documents the whole user-facing path: the four files a caller must supply and the fact that none is ever downloaded, cached, or read from an environment variable; building with the off-by-default `onnxruntime` feature and what a build without it does; provisioning with the pinned digests and byte counts, including that gate `G2` is open so a distributor inherits that gap; single-image, multi-image, digest-verified, and time-budgeted invocations with real output; exit codes; the result schema field by field with its determinism guarantee and its exact-scalar rule; a Rust example using `OcrEngine` with the `!Sync` position stated; every resource limit with the measured figures and the caveat that the benchmark page is synthetic; the whole-input failure model with the reason; and a known-differences section. Earlier, `README.md` was corrected from claiming there was no end-to-end path at all. Two entries in the differences section exist because a user comparing against an older build will see them: recognition now batches in groups of six with a per-batch padded width, and that width truncates rather than rounding up, which shifts confidences around the fourth decimal. The section also records the one place this port deliberately does *less* than upstream — the omitted Clipper union pass — rather than leaving it in a specification document a user will not read. |

Gate P6 (`M2`) requires the approved detector/recognizer path to run end to end
through both Rust API and CLI on the baseline CPU, offline after explicit model
provisioning, without Python or the upstream checkout.

**Status: passed on 2026-08-04.** Evidence in
[`docs/GATE_P6_EVIDENCE.md`](docs/GATE_P6_EVIDENCE.md), which checks each clause
against something that was run. The last clause is demonstrated rather than
asserted: the release binary was copied out of the repository with one PNG and
one manifest and run from that directory under `env -i`, with `ldd` showing only
`libc`, `libm`, `libgcc_s`, and the loader — no Python, and no ONNX Runtime
either, since the runtime is opened at the path given on the command line.
Passing the gate does not make the project releasable: `G2`, `MOD-003`,
`MOD-004`, and everything from P7 onwards remain open.

### P7 — Document input and preprocessing

| ID | Status | Dependencies | Deliverable and acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DOCORI-001` | Done | Gate P6, P3/P4 capability packet | Both halves delivered and every part matched against a capture. **Text-line**: contract, artifact, implementation, bit-identical oracle, a gate against the real model, API and CLI exposure with the default off; on a `180`-degree page it turns `Word` at `0.899` into `World` at `0.9999`. **Document-level**: `resize_short` plus centre crop with both new roundings transcribed from source and all eight captured tensors reproduced bit-identically; all four right angles of the benchmark page identified above `0.92`; `DocumentRotation` reproducing upstream's affine matrix **including its one-pixel offset**, which a plain right-angle rotation would have got wrong across the whole page; the inverse map that returns coordinates to the caller's image; and cubic resampling with `BORDER_CONSTANT`, reproducing all `12` captured rotations **byte for byte**. Two findings are recorded with their measurements: upstream's rotation is one pixel off a true right-angle rotation because its centre is `(w/2, h/2)` rather than `((w-1)/2, (h-1)/2)`, and `src/crop.rs`'s verified cubic warp is not reusable because it replicates the border where this needs constant zero — the difference lands exactly at the expanded canvas's corners. Integration into a pipeline belongs to `DOCPIPE-001`, which owns composing document preprocessing, rather than being bolted onto the classic path. |
| `UNWARP-001` | Done | `DOCORI-001`, P3/P4 capability packet | Contract frozen, `UVDoc` provisioned, implemented with the resource bound stated first — `256 MiB` across both tensors, the decode envelope's precedent, capping a page at `11,184,810` pixels and checked before any allocation. Every input tensor reproduces bit-identically against a capture and every recorded output converts exactly; the comparison immediately caught that `convertTo(CV_8U)` rounds **half to even** via `cvRound`, where the first implementation used Rust's half-away-from-zero `round`. That is the fourth rounding convention pinned in this project and the same one the detector's rescale needed, so the helper is shared. Now reachable through `DOCPIPE-001`, which supplies the answer this item was waiting on: unwarping has no inverse, so `recognize_document` reports `CoordinateSpace::Processed` and `recognize_png` refuses it rather than returning coordinates that look like they belong to the caller's page. |
| `DOCPIPE-001` | Done | `DOCORI-001`, `UNWARP-001` | Composed and running. `src/document_pipeline.rs` carries the coordinate bookkeeping — `CoordinateSpace` names which image a result describes, and `to_source` returns `None` rather than an approximation once an uninvertible stage has run, so a caller must ignore an `Option` to get it wrong. `OcrEngine` loads the two further artifacts optionally, `preprocess_document` runs orientation then unwarping in upstream's order, and a stage enabled without its artifact is a **typed error rather than a silent skip**, because an option that quietly does nothing is worse than one that refuses. `recognize_document` reports the coordinate space; `recognize_png` **refuses** unwarping outright, since its signature has no way to say the coordinates describe another image. Verified with the real models on a page rotated `90` degrees: the text comes back as `["Hello", "World", "Rust", "OCR"]` **and every polygon corner lands inside the caller's `320x800` page** — the second half being the property that shows the inverse was applied rather than forgotten. |
| `DOCIO-DEC-001` | Done | Gate P4, `SCOPE-001` | `docs/ADR_DOCIO_DEC_001_PDF_AND_OFFICE.md` resolves the document half of `D-008`. **Office formats are rejected permanently**, not deferred: an office document is a layout problem rather than an image source, so rendering one requires a word processor, and the text is already present — OCR over a rendering of a `.docx` is a strictly worse way to obtain text that can be read directly. The only case where it is not worse is an embedded scan, which can be extracted without rendering anything. **PDF is in scope and deferred** behind a stated entry gate, because unlike office formats a PDF frequently *is* a scan. Every candidate fails a commitment this project has already made, structurally rather than through immaturity: MuPDF and Poppler are AGPL and GPL, incompatible with the recorded Apache-2.0 distribution position; `pdfium` is permissive but a C++ subsystem larger than this project, needing the same hermetic-rebuild treatment as gate `G2`, which is still open for one much smaller library; pure-Rust renderers are the right shape with unmeasured completeness, and a renderer that silently drops a shading or a CID font produces a plausible wrong image, which this project treats as worse than an error. The ADR is explicit that it decides from licensing and architecture rather than measurement, which is acceptable only because it decides *not* to implement. The policy any implementation must satisfy is recorded now so it constrains the candidate rather than being written to fit the winner: caller-supplied passwords only, never executing or fetching embedded JavaScript, files, or remote references, per-page and whole-document resource bounds reusing the existing budgets, and byte-identical rendering across runs. `PDF-001`'s entry gate requires a compatible licence, a fidelity measurement against a committed corpus in the shape `IMG-003` requires for JPEG, a `G2`-strength supply chain if not pure Rust, a malformed-input corpus, and a recorded partial-page failure decision. |
| `PDF-001` | Blocked | `DOCIO-DEC-001` | Implement the selected bounded PDF renderer; define password, page range/order, DPI, pixel/page/time/memory limits, and unsupported feature errors. Blocked on the five-part entry gate in `docs/ADR_DOCIO_DEC_001_PDF_AND_OFFICE.md`; no candidate satisfies it today. The bounds and the password, embedded-content, and determinism policy this item must implement are already recorded there. |
| `MPAGE-001` | Planned | `PDF-001`, `OCR-001` | Add deterministic multipage execution, page metadata/order, bounded parallelism, cancellation, and partial-failure semantics. |
| `INPUT-001` | Done | `IMG-001`, optional `PDF-001` | `src/input.rs` defines the three supported input shapes — bytes, an explicit local path, and any `std::io::Read` stream — and records why there is no fourth. Writing it exposed a real defect: the CLI used `std::fs::read`, which allocates a file in full and only then meets the `64 MiB` bound, honouring the limit's letter and defeating its purpose. The bound is now enforced **during** the read. A path's declared length is checked first, which rejects an oversized file without reading any of it, but that check is an optimisation and not the guarantee: the streaming read still enforces the bound, because a file can grow between the two and because a special file's metadata may not describe what it will produce. A `200,000,000`-byte file is now refused with a typed `ResourceLimit` naming both figures. Nine tests cover a whole read, one byte at a time, exactly the bound, one past it, an endless stream stopped only by the bound, an empty stream, a missing path, a directory, and a committed fixture read from its path byte-for-byte equal to the embedded copy. `OcrEngine::recognize_path` and `recognize_reader` expose the same bound. **URL input is rejected**, and the reason is recorded rather than left implicit: accepting one makes this project an HTTP client requiring scheme and host allow-listing, redirect chains that can cross into a private network, DNS-rebinding protection, response size and time limits, content validation that does not trust the server's label, and proxy handling — the whole SSRF surface, none of it optional once the first URL is accepted, and all of it redundant when a caller can fetch with a tool built for fetching and pass the bytes. |
| `DOC-E2E-001` | In progress | All above | **The non-PDF slice is done**: `tests/end_to_end.rs` verifies blank, corrupt, and oversized pages through the loaded engine, that a rotated page returns **source** coordinates inside the page, and that unwarping is refused by both `recognize_png` and `detect_png` rather than silently mapped. It found that refusals come in **three** kinds - empty, unsupported format, malformed - and the test now asserts which each case gets. **Remaining**: multipage and password, which are `PDF-001`'s and have no approved renderer. |

Gate P7 (`M3`) requires every promised document input/preprocessing mode to be
bounded, deterministic, and independently documented.

### P8 — Structure and specialized single-model modules

Each module follows the global capability packet: contract, artifact/operator
qualification, implementation, legal fixtures, malformed/boundary tests,
security/license review, docs, and compatibility evidence.

| ID | Status | Dependencies | Deliverable and acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| `LAY-001` | In progress | Gates P3–P7 | Contract frozen from PaddleX `3.7.2`, artifact provisioned, and `src/layout.rs` builds the input tensor, computes the scale factor, and decodes detections with the twenty-class map. **Five** config traps are recorded, four from reading the pinned source and one only a capture could find: `interp: 2` is bicubic; `norm_type: none` is rewritten to `mean_std` so the transform is `x/255`; `target_size` is reversed; and `ToBatch` reverses `img_size` and `scale_factors` **again**, so the model receives `[h, w]` and `[h_scale, w_scale]` — passing the unreversed factor produced boxes reaching `y = 1021` on a `720`-tall page, and the oracle now asserts containment because coordinates outside the source are the cheapest signal a transform is wrong. **An open defect is recorded rather than smoothed over**: the cubic resize matches OpenCV on all five committed small cases and differs by `24` bytes in `1,920,000` at page scale, because OpenCV's 8-bit cubic path is fixed point; two attempts at that arithmetic were *worse* than the float accumulator, so the pass structure was being guessed rather than read and the float version stands with the divergence measured. The fixture marks the affected case `reproduced_exactly: false` and the test bounds it — one 8-bit step, at most two differing samples — so it cannot grow silently. Remaining: the fixed-point cubic path, a gate against the real artifact, and integration, which is `P9`'s subject. **Corrected 2026-08-05**: the v1 oracle transcribed the upstream operators and encoded two bugs - `x / 255` where upstream computes `x * f32(1/255)`, disagreeing on `126` of `256` byte values, and a `BGR` source where `ReadImage(format="RGB")` converts first. Re-captured at schema v2 by the now-committed `tools/capture_layout_oracle.py`, which executes the pinned operators. Two of three cases now exact. |
| `TBLCLS-001` | Done | Gates P3-P4 | `PP-LCNet_x1_0_table_cls` frozen from PaddleX `3.7.2`, artifact provisioned, implemented in `src/table_classification.rs`, all six captured tensors reproduced bit-identically and all four `Topk` cases matched to the bit. Two operators share a name with their C++ namesakes and disagree: `ResizeByShort` rounds half **to even** here against `std::round`'s half away from zero, and `Normalize` folds `scale/std` into a constant instead of dividing last. Not wired into a pipeline - composition is `P9`. See `docs/TABLE_CLASSIFICATION_CONTRACT.md`. |
| `TBLCELL-001` | Done | Gates P3-P4 | Both `RT-DETR-L_*_table_cell_det` models frozen from PaddleX `3.7.2` and implemented in `src/table_cells.rs` over the shared `src/paddlex_detection.rs` path. Three of four captured tensors reproduce bit-identically; the fourth inherits the cubic resize's open divergence and is bounded. Capturing it **found two bugs in `LAY-001`** - a divide where upstream multiplies by a folded `1/255`, and `BGR` where upstream reads `RGB`. Not wired into a pipeline - composition is `P9`. See `docs/TABLE_CELLS_CONTRACT.md`. |
| `TBLSTRUCT-001` | Done | Gates P3-P4 | `SLANeXt_wired` frozen from PaddleX `3.7.2`, artifact provisioned (`368 MB`, the largest here), implemented in `src/table_structure.rs`. All five captured tensors reproduce bit-identically and all five token decodes match exactly. Three facts live in the registration functions rather than the operators - the pad value is `0` not `Pad`'s `127.5`, the config's `scale` is never forwarded, and the pad runs **after** the normalize so the border is a normalized zero rather than a black pixel. A fourth channel order: this model reads **BGR**. Not wired into a pipeline - composition is `P9`. See `docs/TABLE_STRUCTURE_CONTRACT.md`. |
| `FORM-001` | Blocked | Gates P3–P7 | `PP-FormulaNet_plus-M` publishes **no ONNX export**. Blocked under `MODEL-DEC-001`. See `docs/P8_ARTIFACT_AVAILABILITY.md`. |
| `SEAL-001` | Blocked | `DET-004`, `REC-004`, `LAY-001` | `PP-OCRv4_mobile_seal_det` and its server twin publish **no ONNX export** - only Paddle `inference.json` + `inference.pdiparams`. Blocked under `MODEL-DEC-001`, which forbids local conversion. The contract half is readable; the oracle half has nothing to capture against. See `docs/P8_ARTIFACT_AVAILABILITY.md`. |
| `CHART-001` | Blocked | `LAY-001`, `D-010` | `PP-Chart2Table` publishes **no ONNX export** - only `model_state.pdparams`. Blocked under `MODEL-DEC-001`. See `docs/P8_ARTIFACT_AVAILABILITY.md`. |
| `KIE-001` | Blocked | `OCR-001`, `LAY-001` | `PP-DocBee2-3B` publishes **no ONNX export**, and is a vision-language model, so `D-010` blocks it a second time. Blocked under `MODEL-DEC-001`. See `docs/P8_ARTIFACT_AVAILABILITY.md`. |
| `SR-001` | Planned | Gates P3–P4 | Approved text super-resolution model(s), image result contract, quality metric, size/memory bounds, and downstream integration policy. Unblocked by `D-013`: PaddleX `3.7.2` is now a pinned read-only baseline, so this row's operators have a definition to be frozen from. See `docs/PADDLEX_BASELINE.md`. |
| `SPECAPI-001` | In progress | Completed Must modules above | **Table pipeline done**: typed API (`TableEngine`, `TableImage`, `TableResult`), a frozen `paddleocr-rust/table-result/v1` schema with a deterministic writer, and five compatibility rows in `docs/COMPATIBILITY.md` that state plainly what is verified (preprocessing and postprocessing) and what is **not claimed** (detection accuracy). Layout is deliberately **not exposed** while its cubic operator stays one step off; its schema name is frozen and its producer is `pub(crate)`. CLI deferred to `STRUCT-001`, which decides whether there is one structured entry point or several. See `docs/SPECIALIZED_API.md`. |

Gate P8 requires every module classified Must for `M4` to be independently
verified; unimplemented rows remain explicit.

Gate P9 (`M4`) requires evidence-backed output schemas and reconstruction for
every structured pipeline in the approved milestone scope.

### P10 — VLM and GenAI capabilities

| ID | Status | Dependencies | Deliverable and acceptance criteria |

| `VLM-DEC-001` | Blocked by decision | Gates P3/P7, `D-010` | **Evidence packet recorded**, decision outstanding. Upstream has **three** routes - local Paddle, `transformers`, and a **remote GenAI client** - and they have very different answers. Local is blocked **twice**: `PP-DocBee2-3B` is `8.14 GB` with no ONNX export, so `MODEL-DEC-001` blocks it regardless of `D-010`. Remote is cheap in code and expensive in **policy**: it would invert this port's no-network and no-secrets properties. And **neither** fits this repository's compatibility vocabulary - upstream's own code says `temperature` is ignored locally, and *bit-identical* has no meaning for a sampled decoder. See `docs/VLM_DEC_001_EVIDENCE.md`. |
