Gate P10 requires exact model/provider/hardware claims. A remote adapter does
not count as a local native VLM implementation, and neither may masquerade as
the other.

### P11 — API client, serving, deployment, and ecosystem

| ID | Status | Dependencies | Deliverable and acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| `DEPLOY-DEC-001` | Blocked by decision | `SCOPE-001`, Gates P6/P10 as applicable | **Evidence packet recorded**, decision outstanding. Measured, not estimated: `wasm32-unknown-unknown` **type-checks today** at default features and fails only with `onnxruntime`, because `ort`'s `load-dynamic` has no `wasm32` backend; `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` type-checks; the release CLI is `361,288` bytes over `8` dependencies. The C ABI obstacle is `13` `String`s and `3` `PathBuf`s needing an ownership contract, not surface size. The eight targets are **not equally distant**. No recommendation offered - `D-012` is a scope decision. See `docs/DEPLOY_DEC_001_EVIDENCE.md`. |
| `CLIENT-001` | Planned | `DEPLOY-DEC-001`, P2 contracts | If in scope, typed sync/async Rust cloud client for submit/poll/batch/results/resources with timeouts, cancellation, auth redaction, URL/path safety, atomic saves, and mocked offline tests. |
| `SERVER-001` | Planned | Gate P6, `DEPLOY-DEC-001` | If in scope, versioned local HTTP service/OpenAPI with bounded uploads/jobs, cancellation, backpressure, auth/network policy, health, errors, and schema parity. |
| `OBS-001` | Done | `SERVER-001` or `OCR-003` | Unblocked by `OCR-003`, which the **or** in this dependency satisfies. `src/observability.rs` makes leaking **structurally impossible**: `StageEvent` has nowhere to put a document - a `'static` stage label, a duration, and counts, with no `String`, no `PathBuf`, and no generic payload. Cardinality is fixed at compile time because `Stage` is a closed enum. Renders deterministically in pipeline order, bounds its event count and **counts** what it drops, and records that a stage failed without recording the error - whose payload is where a path would arrive. Overhead measured, not asserted. |
| `ACCEL-001` | Planned | `RT-004`, `D-012` | Qualify each optional GPU/accelerator/backend independently through raw tensor, component, E2E, determinism, security, license, and platform gates. |
| `CABI-001` | Planned | `API-001`, `D-012` | If in scope, stable C ABI with ownership, buffers, errors, versioning, panic containment, examples, and ABI tests. |
| `WASM-001` | Planned | `API-001`, `D-012` | If in scope, browser/WASM worker and model-loading path with web resource limits, CSP/origin/cache policy, and verified compatible models. |
| `MOBILE-001` | Planned | `CABI-001` or target-native decision | If in scope, Android/iOS packaging/bindings and lifecycle/thread/memory tests; duplicating upstream demo UI is not automatic scope. |
| `DEPLOY-001` | Planned | Approved server/CLI targets | Reproducible release/container packages, configuration, offline model mounts, least privilege, health/shutdown, notices, SBOM, and checksums. |
| `ECO-001` | Planned | `SERVER-001`/`CLIENT-001` | Classify and, if approved, provide MCP/LangChain or equivalent Rust/interoperability integrations with security and contract tests. |

Gate P11 (`M5`) requires every promised integration/deployment target to have an
explicit support matrix and release-quality evidence.

### P12 — Native training, evaluation, export, and optimization

This track is separate from inference delivery. It may begin after P3 only when
`D-011` is resolved and cannot destabilize the verified inference API.

| ID | Status | Dependencies | Deliverable and acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| `TRAIN-DEC-001` | Blocked by decision | `D-011`, `SCOPE-002` | **Evidence packet recorded**, decision outstanding. The surface is measured, not estimated: **`121`** distinct architecture components across the `139` configs - `36` backbones, `33` losses, `31` heads, `16` necks, `5` transforms - plus `4` optimizers and `8` schedules. Training only the two `Verified` configs needs **`4`**. That is the actual shape of `D-011`: not whether to port training, but which of those two numbers. See `docs/TRAIN_DEC_001_EVIDENCE.md`. |
| `DATA-001` | Planned | `TRAIN-DEC-001` | Implement approved simple/multiscale/LMDB/PGNet/PubTab/LaTeX dataset contracts, deterministic shuffling, safe parsing, bounded records, and worker behavior. |
| `AUG-001` | Planned | `DATA-001`, P4 geometry | Implement scoped augmentations and label encoders with seeded determinism and golden geometry/label tests. |
| `ARCH-DET-001` | Planned | `TRAIN-DEC-001` | Implement and validate in-scope detection/e2e Transform→Backbone→Neck→Head configurations against raw activations/parameters. |
| `ARCH-REC-001` | Planned | `TRAIN-DEC-001` | Implement and validate in-scope recognition/formula configurations. |
| `ARCH-STRUCT-001` | Planned | `TRAIN-DEC-001` | Implement and validate in-scope classification/table/KIE/SR configurations. |
| `LOSS-001` | Planned | Architecture scope | Implement scoped losses with forward and gradient comparisons, invalid-shape handling, and numerical stability tests. |
| `METRIC-001` | In progress | Architecture/data scope | **Detection and recognition halves done**: `src/metrics.rs` implements `DetectionIoUEvaluator`, `DetMetric`, and `RecMetric` frozen from the pinned PaddleOCR checkout, every captured case matched. Includes convex-polygon IoU by Sutherland-Hodgman, the greedy index-order matcher, the strictly-greater `0.5` threshold, and the `1e-5` epsilon that keeps a perfect run just under `1.0`. **Remaining**: table, KIE, and SR metrics, which score modules this port does not have; distributed reduction, which has nothing to reduce across. See `docs/METRIC_CONTRACT.md`. |
| `OPT-001` | Planned | `LOSS-001` | Implement approved optimizers, regularizers, schedulers, gradient clipping, accumulation, EMA, AMP, and deterministic seed policy. |
| `LOOP-001` | Planned | Data/architecture/loss/metric/optimizer items | Implement bounded train/eval loops, checkpoint/resume, logging, validation cadence, cancellation, distributed/mixed-precision scope, and failure recovery. |
| `CKPT-001` | Planned | `LOOP-001` | Define safe versioned native checkpoints and approved Paddle import/conversion with shape/name/provenance validation; never deserialize unsafe arbitrary objects. |
| `EXPORTMODEL-001` | Planned | `CKPT-001`, `MOD-002` | Export verified inference artifacts/manifests with reproducible conversion and training-to-inference parity tests. |
| `SLIM-001` | Planned | `EXPORTMODEL-001` | If in scope, distillation, pruning, quantization, and compression with measured accuracy/size/performance budgets. |
| `CONFIG-001` | Done | All training components | Every one of the **`139`** configs in the pinned checkout is classified per file: **`2` Verified**, `84` *postprocess implemented, parameters unverified*, `53` Deferred. The roadmap estimated `~155`; the measured count is recorded instead. `Out of scope` is deliberately **unassigned** - it is a user-approved exclusion, not one this reconciliation may make. A test enforces the row's own constraint: no config may be `Verified` merely for sharing a postprocess with one that is. See `docs/CONFIG_RECONCILIATION.md`. |

Gate P12 (`M6`) is reached only for the algorithm/config rows individually
verified in `docs/COMPATIBILITY.md`. Partial training coverage must be named precisely.

### P13 — Security, reliability, licensing, performance, and platforms

These concerns are implemented in every prior phase; P13 is the final audit and
hardening gate, not their first appearance.

| ID | Status | Dependencies | Deliverable and acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| `THREAT-001` | Done | Gates P6–P12 as applicable | `docs/THREAT_MODEL.md`. For a project this size the model is mostly a list of surfaces that **do not exist**, and saying so is more useful than inventing threats to look thorough: a model describing attacks on features the code lacks obscures the ones against features it has. Two trust boundaries are identified — the encoded image, hostile by assumption, and the artifacts, verified only when the caller declares a digest. Decoder threats are tabulated against their mitigations, each of which is a bound enforced before the allocation it governs. The artifact section states the counter-intuitive property plainly: the detector and recognizer are **not distinguishable by shape**, so a substituted model with the same ABI is caught by identity or not at all, which makes optional digests a documented residual rather than a mitigated one. Whole classes are recorded as inapplicable with the reason — no server, no archive extraction, no PDF, no VLM, no training, no deserialization framework, no environment-based configuration. One finding came out of writing it: two tests wrote to a fixed name in the world-writable temporary directory, where another user could pre-create the path as a symlink for `fs::write` to follow. Test-only and low severity, but exactly the pattern the model exists to catch; both now build a process-unique path. Four open risks are listed and none is closed by asserting it is small. |
| `FUZZ-001` | Done | Pure processing surfaces | `src/fuzz.rs` and the feature-gated `fuzz-primitives` stdin target drive checked public types, bounded DB, CTC, geometry, and crop kernels, and — added under `ROB-001` — the four surfaces that consume caller-supplied bytes directly: the PNG decoder, the manifest parser, the bounded stream reader, and the dictionary parser. The driver performs no I/O, no allocation derived from an unbounded input length, no model loading, no inference, and no result serialization. A `4,000`-case seeded campaign runs clean in both release and debug-with-overflow-checks builds; `docs/ROB_001_EVIDENCE.md` records the generators, the results, and the fact that generation-driven fuzzing explores shallowly compared with a coverage-guided run. |
| `ROB-001` | Done | All runtime/model modules | `docs/ROB_001_EVIDENCE.md` verifies every clause and cites where. Writing it exposed that the fuzz driver was covering the wrong half of the attack surface: it drove the checked public types and the DB, CTC, geometry, and crop kernels, all of which take *derived* values the pipeline produced, and drove **no** surface that consumes caller-supplied bytes directly — which is where a hostile document actually arrives. Four were added: the PNG decoder, the manifest parser reached through a lossy UTF-8 conversion so arbitrary bytes get past the encoding check, the bounded stream reader driven by a reader that hands out irregular pieces rather than one slice, and the dictionary parser. A `4,000`-case campaign — uniform random bytes, bit-flip mutations and truncations of the committed fixtures, and synthetic PNG headers with hostile declared dimensions, invalid bit depths, and invalid colour types — ran clean in both `--release` and a debug build with overflow checks and `debug_assertions`. The debug run is the one that matters for arithmetic: release wraps integer overflow silently, so a wrong-by-overflow index in the header arithmetic would have surfaced only there. Recorded limits: the campaign is generation-driven rather than coverage-guided, so it explores shallowly, and `4,000` cases is a smoke test rather than a campaign. |
| `SAFE-001` | Done | All native/unsafe dependencies | `docs/SAFE_001_AUDIT.md` audits the one native boundary and the whole dependency graph in both feature configurations, with counts read from the vendored sources rather than taken from any crate's description of itself. This crate contains **no unsafe code**: both roots use `forbid(unsafe_code)` rather than `deny`, so it cannot be re-enabled by an inner `allow` without editing the crate root. That reduces the memory-safety argument entirely to dependencies and one dynamic library, which is what the audit then scrutinises. The graph is one direct dependency plus eight transitive, four more with `onnxruntime`; `png` itself contains zero `unsafe`, and every licence in the graph is permissive — MIT, Apache-2.0, ISC, 0BSD, or Zlib, no copyleft anywhere — which is what makes the `MODEL-DEC-001` distribution position tenable. The whole graph contains **one** build script, `crc32fast`'s twenty-seven lines, which reads `rustc --version` and emits one `cfg`. `ort-sys` has none, and that absence is the most checkable evidence that `RT-004`'s central claim holds: with `load-dynamic` there is nothing to find, download, or link at build time. The native boundary is mediated by `run_validated`, which checks name, rank, per-axis extents, and element budgets inbound and the actual returned name, shape, and finiteness outbound. Exception containment was verified behaviourally rather than by reading: a model pair that loads and cannot run returns a typed backend error and exit `2`, with no abort, panic, or partial result. Two things are recorded as **not closed**: gate `G2`, and the fact that dependency `unsafe` was counted rather than reviewed line by line — `375` sites in `ort` alone is not an amount this audit read individually, and claiming otherwise would be exactly the overstatement this repository refuses. |
| `LIC-002` | Done | All dependencies/assets | `docs/LIC_002_AUDIT.md` resolves every asset's licence. The structural result is that the audit is short because this project bundles nothing: no model weights, no dictionary, no ONNX Runtime library, no fonts, no datasets, and no binary blob of any kind — `git ls-files` matches no `.ttf`, `.so`, `.onnx`, `.tar`, or `.zip`. Every committed file is text, and all twenty fixture inputs declare `Apache-2.0` with a provenance and a consuming test the integrity gate enforces. The dependency graph is entirely permissive with **no copyleft in either feature configuration**, which is what makes the `MODEL-DEC-001` distribution position tenable and why `DOCIO-DEC-001` rejects the AGPL and GPL PDF renderers. Two findings were fixed during the audit rather than deferred. Eight of thirty-nine Rust files carried no SPDX header — hygiene rather than exposure, since all are project-authored, but a per-file identifier is what makes an automated scan agree with the repository's own claim; all thirty-nine now carry one. And `NOTICE` under-attributed: it credited OpenCV for `src/contour.rs` alone when nine modules derive observable behaviour from a third-party implementation, including `src/unclip.rs` from Clipper via pyclipper under the Boost Software License, a different project entirely. Under-attributing a behavioural derivation is a real defect in a project whose entire method is reading other implementations closely; the notice now lists each module against the specific upstream file or algorithm it came from. |
| `PERF-001` | Done | `QUAL-001`, each milestone | `docs/PERF_001_BENCHMARK.md` records the full configuration — hardware, thread policy, toolchain, backend, all four artifact digests with byte counts, corpus, warmup, and network — and adds the two dimensions `G3` did not measure. **Startup** is `0.69 s`, derived as total minus twenty times the marginal page cost, and cross-checks against the independently measured `0.534 s` in-process engine load. **Throughput** at steady state is `0.357` pages per second single threaded, from a `20`-page marginal cost of `2.80 s` that matches the separately measured warm median of `2.840 s`. Both the `5`-page and `20`-page figures are reported because quoting only the first understates throughput and only the second hides how long the fixed cost takes to amortise. Two findings come from the multi-page run specifically: peak RSS rises from the single-image figure as the runtime's arenas reach working size and then **plateaus** — `608,964 kB` at five pages, `607,488 kB` at twenty — so twenty pages through one engine accumulate nothing, which a single-image measurement cannot show; and all twenty emitted documents are byte-identical, adding a long single-process run to the determinism evidence. |
| `PERF-002` | Done | `PERF-001` | Every predeclared budget is met and **none was amended**. The verdict table judges peak memory against the *worst* figure observed anywhere in the record — the `594.7 MiB` multi-image run rather than the more convenient single-image one — because a budget should be judged against the least favourable measurement taken. Startup and throughput are recorded with **no budget attached**, deliberately: writing a threshold after seeing the number is exactly what `QUALITY_PROFILE.md` forbids, and a budget invented to fit a passing measurement proves nothing. The limits of the comparison are recorded with the same care as the figures: one host, one synthetic page that is a lower bound on real scans, single threaded by construction, and no comparison against upstream, because a wall-clock comparison across two runtimes and an interpreter would measure those rather than this port's arithmetic. |
| `CONC-001` | Done | Pipeline/service/training scope | `docs/CONC_001_EVIDENCE.md`. Several of this item's clauses have the answer "there is none, deliberately", and saying so is more useful than inventing a mechanism to describe: this project spawns no threads, has no worker pool, queue, scheduler, or async runtime, and pins both sessions to one intra-op and one inter-op thread — measured at `99%` of one CPU. Thread safety is enforced by the compiler rather than documented: `OcrEngine` holds its sessions in a `RefCell`, so it is `!Sync` and cannot be shared across threads, and an auto-trait probe asserts that property so a future lock cannot silently convert a compile error into a hidden queue. Determinism under concurrency is measured at full precision: four threads, each with its own engine and three runs, produce twelve **serialized documents byte-identical** to the single-threaded baseline. Comparing documents rather than text is deliberate — two runs can agree on every string while differing in a coordinate or the last digit of a confidence, and the serialized form is what a caller writing to disk depends on. Allowed numerical variation is **none**, with the condition under which that stops being true stated in advance: enabling multiple intra-op threads makes reduction order thread-count dependent, and the tolerance would have to be declared before that change rather than after seeing what it produces. Cancellation, the stage-boundary limit, and clean shutdown are recorded with their evidence. |
| `PLAT-001` | Planned | `D-003`, `D-012` | Run applicable unit/integration/model smoke/package tests on every promised OS/architecture/backend; Rust portability alone is not platform evidence. |
| `SUPPLY-001` | Done | Release dependencies | `docs/SUPPLY_001_POLICY.md` and `sbom/paddleocr-rust.cdx.json`. The SBOM is CycloneDX `1.5`, **generated** from `Cargo.lock` by `tools/generate_sbom.py`, listing all `48` locked packages with `purl`, licence expression, source, and the SHA-256 of the published `.crate` archive. It is generated rather than written because a hand-maintained SBOM goes stale on the first dependency change, and a stale SBOM is worse than none: it asserts a composition that is no longer true. `tests/supply_chain.rs` makes drift a build failure, requiring exact correspondence in both directions plus matching versions and checksums — and the check was verified to actually fail by removing one component, because a test never seen to fail is not yet evidence. Licences are checked against an explicit list of nine reviewed expressions rather than a permissive-by-pattern rule, so a new expression fails and gets looked at even when it is obviously fine, which is exactly the judgement a review exists to make. Every direct dependency is enforced to carry an exact `=` pin, which is what makes the audited graph the built graph. Checksums are recorded in three layers, and the gap is stated: this project verifies the models it loads and does **not** verify the runtime library it loads them into. Signing is deliberately absent — it is meaningful at a distribution boundary this project has not crossed, and adopting a scheme now would mean choosing key custody for a distribution that does not exist; `P14` needs it covering the binary, the SBOM, and the manifest together, since a signed binary with an unsigned manifest leaves artifact identity unprotected. No advisory scan runs offline, and the shape of the graph rather than a scanner is what currently bounds the exposure. |

Gate P13 requires no unresolved critical security, licensing, correctness, or
platform finding; no known panic/hang/unbounded path for covered untrusted input;
and met or explicitly re-approved quality/performance budgets.

### P14 — Compatibility closeout and release qualification

| ID | Status | Dependencies | Deliverable and acceptance criteria |
|---|---|---|---|
| `CLOSE-001` | Done | Gates P6–P13 | `docs/CLOSE_001_AUDIT.md` classifies all `121` inventory rows into the four required buckets with nothing left unclassified: `7` verified, `6` approved intentional differences, the large remainder deferred to a **named** milestone with its blocking condition, and `3` classes out of scope. `docs/COMPATIBILITY.md` was materially stale — its status line still read "M2 planning ledger; no row is implemented or verified" while all seven `Must` rows were implemented and gate-verified — and each row now carries its evidence rather than an intention, satisfying that ledger's own transition rule of a contract, a fixture, a tolerance, and a reproducing test. Two intentional differences that had accumulated during M2 were promoted out of module documentation into the ledger where they belong: PNG-only input, and the omitted Clipper union pass, which is the one place this port deliberately does *less* than upstream. The audit ends by refusing to flatter the result: reading the inventory in one sitting is a corrective to a change log full of green, because this project has verified one vertical slice for one artifact pair and one image format, which is a small fraction of what the inventory lists, and each remaining phase is comparable in size to the one completed. `CLOSE-001` closes because every row is classified, not because the work is done. |
| `STABLE-001` | Done | `CLOSE-001` | `docs/STABLE_001_API_REVIEW.md` found one systemic problem and fixed it. Three public types grew during this milestone — `Error` gained `TimedOut`, `OcrOptions` gained `control`, `result_to_json` gained two parameters — and **every one would have been a breaking change after `1.0`**, because a public enum without `#[non_exhaustive]` cannot gain a variant and a public struct without it cannot gain a field. The API had no mechanism to grow; nothing broke only because nothing depends on it yet, which is luck rather than design and would have expired at the first release. `#[non_exhaustive]` is now on nine types, and because it forbids struct literals, `OcrOptions::with_*` and `Artifacts::new` plus `with_*_sha256` were added so callers are not stuck — with `Artifacts::new` documenting what omitting a digest means rather than letting the absence look like a default. The review records positions on names, defaults, ownership, errors, feature flags, backend neutrality, a semver policy that names what is **not** covered (`fuzz`, `digest`, and `result_json`'s signature, whose stable contract is the document shape and the CLI's `--json` rather than the Rust function), deprecations, and the CLI as a scripting contract that can break without any Rust signature moving. Three things are left explicitly unsettled, including that `Score` and `Polygon` are public but unused by the OCR API. |
| `DOC-FINAL-001` | Done | `STABLE-001` | The documentation set covers installation and building, offline provisioning with digests, CLI and Rust examples, formats and languages, compatibility, limits, security, architecture, contribution, and the release evidence documents. The gap this item closed was **troubleshooting**, which did not exist: a user hitting an error had the reference material to work out what it meant and nothing that simply told them. `docs/USER_GUIDE.md` §9 now groups nine failure modes by what the reader should change rather than by which module produced them, and every quoted message was checked against the sources — one was wrong and was corrected to the string the code actually emits, which is the failure mode a hand-written troubleshooting section has. Two entries carry information available nowhere else: that a swapped detector and recognizer **load** and fail only on first use, and that an empty result is never a timeout because an expired budget returns an error, so the two can be told apart. A link check across all `239` relative markdown links in the repository found none broken. |
| `CLEAN-001` | Done | Gates P13, `DOC-FINAL-001` | `docs/CLEAN_001_EVIDENCE.md`. A fresh `git clone` into a directory with no upstream symlink, no Python environment, and none of this working copy's files: build, `253` tests on default features, `262` on all features, docs, package, release build with the backend, an end-to-end OCR run under `env -i` producing correct text for two images, and gate `G1` reproducing all four fixtures — every step offline. "Did not use the upstream checkout" is a property of that environment rather than a claim, because the symlink does not exist in it. One doc warning was found and fixed: `control`'s public documentation linked to the private `crate::pipeline`, a public page pointing somewhere a reader cannot follow. |
| `PKG-001` | Done | `CLEAN-001`, `SUPPLY-001` | The package contained files that must never ship, and finding it is the point of the item. Run in the working copy, `cargo package` produced `232` files including a user-supplied image, an unrelated user file, the agent directory, **`34` files walked through the read-only upstream symlink**, and `18` from a local Python virtual environment. Cargo collects licence and notice files by name from anywhere in the tree, and that walk followed the symlink; `.gitignore` did not prevent it, because the licence collection does not respect it. A release built that way would have redistributed upstream and third-party licence files in direct conflict with `MODEL-DEC-001`. `Cargo.toml` now carries an explicit `include` list, so package contents are **stated** rather than inherited from whatever happens to be in the working directory. Verified from the clean checkout: `176` files, `2.4 MiB`, entirely text, with no `.onnx`, `.so`, `target/`, credential, or user file, and with `LICENSE`, `NOTICE`, the SBOM carrying a SHA-256 per dependency, `Cargo.lock`, and `tests/supply_chain.rs` so a consumer can re-run the drift check. The consequence is recorded as a procedure: a release **must** be built from a clean checkout, because `include` does not govern the files cargo collects by name and `.gitignore` does not stop the walk — either safeguard alone is insufficient. |
| `RC-001` | Done | All above | `docs/RC_001_RELEASE_EVIDENCE.md`, produced from one fresh clone at commit `86857e7` with a clean tree: toolchain and target, artifact hashes and byte counts, every offline and provisioned command with its result, the compatibility summary, the benchmark table with verdicts, the six security and licence reviews, and ten known limitations stated plainly. Two corrections to earlier documents are carried: the release binary is now `853,104` bytes rather than `812,144`, grown by the modules added since `G3`, and `cargo package` was run **with** verification, closing the `--no-verify` caveat `CLEAN-001` had recorded. One real finding: running the ignored library gates the default way — all of them, in parallel — produces five failures inside `ort`'s mutex, because several initialise the process-global ONNX Runtime environment concurrently, and one of them must run in its own process before any successful initialisation. Every earlier run in this project invoked them individually by name, which hid the constraint entirely. It is a property of the runtime rather than a defect in the tests, and the three exact commands are now recorded in `CONTRIBUTING.md` where a release runner will look. The document ends by refusing to close what it cannot: `USER-GATE-001` and `REL-001` need the user, and `publish = false` remains set. |
| `USER-GATE-001` | Answered 2026-08-04 | `RC-001` | The user was asked, with the delivered scope and the unimplemented phases both stated plainly, whether M2 is the finish line. **Answer: continue into P7 and beyond.** M2 is therefore not the release scope and this gate does not close; it reopens as the question to ask again when a later milestone is a candidate. Recorded here as the decision it is, per the roadmap-discipline rule that a material scope answer must be written down rather than inferred. |
| `REL-001` | Blocked by decision | `USER-GATE-001` | The user was asked to authorise external effects — tagging, packaging for distribution, or publishing. **Answer: no external effects.** Nothing is tagged, packaged, or published; `publish = false` stays set in `Cargo.toml`; the only thing that has left this repository is commits to the user's own `main`. `docs/RC_001_RELEASE_EVIDENCE.md` remains ready for whenever that answer changes. |

Gate P14 (`M7`) is the only gate that can formally complete this roadmap.

## 11. Project Definition of Finished

This roadmap is finished only when all of the following are true:

1. Gates P0 through P14 are complete for the user-approved pinned-baseline
   target in `SCOPE-002`.
2. Every required inventory row is `Verified`; no row is silently deleted,
   weakened, or relabeled during final review. Any `Out of scope` row has an
   explicit user-approved rationale and is not implied by public claims.
3. At least one exact, useful detector-plus-recognizer family runs end to end on
   every promised baseline CPU target through both native Rust API and CLI.
4. Build, normal tests, docs, packages, installation, and runtime require no
   Python, PaddleOCR/PaddleX FFI, upstream checkout, network, or GPU.
5. Model provisioning is explicit, checksummed, reproducible, license-reviewed,
   and offline-capable after provisioning.
6. Geometry, ordering, text, confidence, defaults, errors, JSON schema, models,
   languages, and intentional differences are documented and verified against
   the exact upstream baseline.
7. All in-scope modules, composite pipelines, VLM/service/deployment targets,
   and training/config families satisfy the global capability Definition of
   Done individually.
8. Malformed input and resource-limit behavior are tested, and no unresolved
   critical security, licensing, correctness, reliability, or platform issue
   remains.
9. Accuracy/parity and performance budgets pass on named reference systems or
   were changed before release through an evidence-backed, user-approved
   roadmap amendment.
10. A clean release candidate passes every supported-platform gate and carries
    complete compatibility, provenance, notices, checksums, and known limits.
11. User documentation makes no unsupported full-parity, model, language,
    platform, deployment, training, or benchmark claim.
12. The user explicitly confirms the frozen completion scope and final evidence.

“Finished” applies to the exact pinned and approved baseline, not every future
PaddleOCR release. Full PaddleOCR parity may be stated only if every capability
in that baseline inventory is individually verified. A future upstream release,
model, platform, or accelerator starts a new versioned milestone; it does not
silently reopen or redefine a completed one.

## 12. Evidence and change log

| Date | Version/item | Change or evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-08-05 | `VLM-DEC-001` evidence: three routes, three different answers | The third decision packet, recorded the same way as `D-012`'s and `D-011`'s. Upstream defines **three** predictors, not one: local Paddle dynamic graph, Hugging Face `transformers`, and a **remote GenAI client** - and upstream treats the remote adapter as a first-class route rather than a fallback, which means a port could implement the adapter and none of the local inference. That is a materially smaller project than `D-010` reads like. The **local** route is blocked **twice**: `PP-DocBee2-3B` is `8.14 GB` across nine files and publishes no ONNX export, so `MODEL-DEC-001` blocks it regardless of what `D-010` decides - and the second block is the one `D-010` does not control. The **remote** route needs no artifact, no tensor framework, and no GPU; it is small in code and large in **policy**, because it inverts two properties this port currently gets by construction: no outbound connections, and no credential handling - the very thing `OBS-001` was built to make impossible to leak. And a finding that applies to both: this repository's compatibility vocabulary - *bit-identical*, *byte for byte*, *reproduced* - **has no meaning for a sampled decoder**. Upstream's own code even says `temperature` is ignored by the local model. `D-010` would need a different vocabulary before it could need an implementation. No recommendation offered. |
| 2026-08-05 | `TRAIN-DEC-001` evidence: `121` components for full coverage, `4` for the minimal one | The row asks for a strategy selected "by operator coverage", and coverage **of what** is the question. Counted across the `139` configs rather than estimated: `36` backbones, `33` losses, `31` heads, `16` necks, `5` transforms - **`121`** distinct architecture components - plus `4` optimizers and `8` learning-rate schedules. Training only the two configs `CONFIG-001` marks `Verified` needs **four**: `PPLCNetV4`, `DBHead`, `MultiHead`, `RepLKPAN`, with `DBLoss`, `MultiLoss`, `Adam`, and `Cosine`. So `D-011` is not "port training or not" - it is **which of those two numbers** the project signs up for. Recorded alongside: `17` dataset transforms that are not autograd at all but augmentation and label encoding, several of them (`MakeShrinkMap`, `MakeBorderMap`, `RandomPerspective`) the same geometry this port already implements for **inference**, run in the opposite direction. What is genuinely missing is **gradients** - an operator that agrees with Paddle on a forward pass can disagree on a backward one, and nothing here currently tests one. Three existing constraints bear on the choice and are named: `unsafe_code = "forbid"` against tensor frameworks that need it, `8` dependencies and a `361 KB` binary against a framework's tree, and the capture-comparison method that would have to extend to numerical gradient checks. No recommendation offered. |
| 2026-08-05 | `DEPLOY-DEC-001` evidence: the eight targets are not equally distant | `D-012` is a scope decision and stays the user's, so this does for it what `IMG-003`'s gate did for JPEG - measures what is true so the decision is made against facts. The finding that changes the picture: **`wasm32-unknown-unknown` type-checks today** at default features. Everything this port does without a model - PNG decoding, geometry, DB postprocessing, CTC, table composition, reading order, Markdown, metrics, resolution, observability - is already WASM-portable, and nothing was done to make it so. It fails **only** with `onnxruntime`, and the errors name the cause exactly: `ort`'s `load-dynamic` has no `wasm32` implementation, because there is no dynamic library to load in a browser. So the WASM question is not "is this crate portable" but "is a second runtime backend acceptable" - and `MODEL-DEC-001`'s reasoning about extra verification surfaces applies to backends too. `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` also type-checks, which is recorded as a **starting point and not a Windows claim**: `PLAT-001` asks for tests run, and a type-check is not a run. The C ABI obstacle turns out not to be surface size - `75` functions is manageable - but `13` `String`s and `3` `PathBuf`s that each need an ownership contract, plus the fact that `#[non_exhaustive]` does not survive a C header. And the `361,288`-byte binary over `8` dependencies is recorded as an asset that the service and ecosystem targets would each spend. |
| 2026-08-05 | `CONFIG-001` done: `139` configs classified per file, and only two Verified | The row's hardest requirement is not the classification - it is **"no generic family claim from one config"**. It would have been easy to mark every `DBPostProcess` config `Verified` after matching one, and it would have been false. So exactly **two** files are `Verified` - the two whose parameters this port froze and matched against a capture - and the other `84` configs whose postprocess this port implements are *postprocess implemented, **parameters unverified***, which is a different and true statement. `53` are Deferred. A test enforces the constraint directly: for each `Verified` config it finds every other config sharing its postprocess and asserts none of them is also `Verified`, so a future change that promoted a family fails. `Out of scope` is **left unassigned on purpose** - the roadmap reserves it for a user-approved exclusion, and a reconciliation records evidence rather than making that decision; a test asserts the status never appears. Two corrections to my own numbers: the checkout holds **`139`** configs, not the `~155` this roadmap estimated, and the measured count is what is recorded. And the record is committed as a fixture rather than scanned at test time, because the upstream checkout is a read-only symlink a clean checkout does not have - one test compares record against checkout **when it is present**, which is what stops the record from drifting. |
| 2026-08-05 | `METRIC-001` detection and recognition halves: the accuracy question becomes askable | Every compatibility row here says **no accuracy claim**, which is honest and is also a gap: without upstream's own metric, "this port agrees with upstream" and "this port is as good as upstream" cannot be asked as separate questions. `src/metrics.rs` closes the tooling half, frozen from `ppocr/metrics/` in the **PaddleOCR** checkout rather than PaddleX. Three behaviours a reimplementation would get wrong by accident. The matcher is **greedy in index order, not best-first** - the captured case has two detections for one region with the *second* an exact match, and the **first** is the one that matches, which halves precision. The IoU threshold is **strictly greater**, so a detection at exactly `0.5` does not match; a pair whose IoU is exactly `0.5` pins that boundary. And `RecMetric` divides by `total + 1e-5`, so **a perfect run scores `0.99999`, not `1.0`** - reproducing that is the difference between agreeing with upstream and being almost right. Two details this port had to get right on its own: edit distance counts **characters**, since comparing UTF-8 bytes would score one CJK substitution as three edits, and polygon intersection is Sutherland-Hodgman with a winding-order correction. Also recorded rather than tidied: `is_filter` **erases non-Latin text entirely**, so two different CJK strings compare equal under it. |
| 2026-08-05 | `OBS-001` done, found by actually scanning the `Planned` rows | I said I would rescan for unblocked slices, and this is what it produced. `OBS-001`'s dependency is `SERVER-001` **or** `OCR-003` - an alternative my first survey read as a conjunction, so it looked blocked and was not. Its requirement is observability that does not leak document text, credentials, URLs, or paths. The usual way to meet that is a **rule** plus review, and rules decay; so `StageEvent` has **nowhere to put** any of them - a `'static` stage label, a duration, and counts, with no `String`, no `PathBuf`, no generic payload. A future field that could carry one would be a visible change to a public struct rather than a slip inside a format string. Cardinality is fixed at compile time because `Stage` is a closed enum, so a caller cannot create one metric series per document. Failures record **that** a stage failed and not the error, because an error's payload is exactly where a path arrives. The render is deterministic in pipeline order rather than arrival order, so a diff shows a real change and not a scheduling artefact. One defect caught during the work: `dropped()` returned a constant `0` while its own documentation said overflow was counted - the doc was written and the counter was not. |
| 2026-08-05 | `DOC-E2E-001` non-PDF slice, and a correction to my own status claim | The previous entry said this session had exhausted the work that needs no user decision. **That was too strong.** `DOC-E2E-001` lists rotated, warped, multipage, empty, corrupt, password, and oversized cases; only multipage and password are `PDF-001`'s, and the rest go through document preprocessing, which is implemented and `Done`. Those are now verified against real artifacts: a blank page yields zero lines rather than an error, a rotated page returns **`Source`** coordinates that lie inside the page, and unwarping is refused by both `recognize_png` and `detect_png` rather than silently mapped - the boundary `docs/UNWARPING_CONTRACT.md` section 3 names. Writing it found that this port's refusals come in **three** kinds where the test first assumed two: **empty** for no bytes, **unsupported** for a signature this port does not handle, and **invalid** for a PNG that claims to be one and is not. Collapsing them would lose the only signal telling a caller whether to send something, convert it, or re-fetch it - so the test asserts which kind each case gets rather than accepting any refusal. |
| 2026-08-05 | `IMG-003` entry gate measured: a delta of `36` changed no character | The roadmap made measuring the **consequence** of the recorded `36`-component JPEG decoder delta the precondition for deciding anything about JPEG. That measurement now exists. The probe perturbs an already-decoded page rather than using a JPEG decoder, which isolates "what does a delta of `d` do" from "which decoder produces it", and runs the whole pipeline so the number measured is the effect on **output**. On the committed benchmark page with the M2 pair: recognized text **identical** in all five cases including scattered `+/-36`, line count identical, worst corner movement `1` px uniform and `4` px scattered. Three limits are recorded as loudly as the result, because they are what stops this from being an argument for accepting JPEG. **One page** - six short high-contrast lines, where a dense small-text scan is the regime that would actually stress the detector's threshold. **The perturbation is not a decoder difference** - real JPEG disagreement is spatially correlated along the `8x8` block grid and clusters at edges, which is where text is, and scattered noise averages out under a resize in a way block error does not. And **no accuracy claim**: "same text" means the two runs agreed, not that either was right. The row moves to `Blocked by decision` with the gate satisfied and no recommendation offered - the choice trades byte-exactness against input coverage, which is not a technical question. |
| 2026-08-05 | `MODAPI-001` done: detection without recognition | `OcrEngine::detect_png` runs the detector and the reading-order sort and stops, which costs the recognizer's whole run less than `recognize_png` - on a dense page, most of it. Verified against real artifacts rather than asserted: on the committed benchmark page the two paths agree on **six regions, the same boxes, in the same order**, because both run the same sort. Two deliberate refusals. **`drop_score` is not applied**: it filters on *recognition* confidence, and there is none here, so reusing it against the detector's score would silently mean something else. And unwarping is refused, for the reason `recognize_png` refuses it. The score is the **detector's**, and the schema names the field `detector_score` rather than `confidence` so a consumer cannot compare it with `ocr-result/v1`'s by accident - two different numbers with different ranges of meaning. Implemented as a **stage method on the loaded engine** rather than a new engine type, for the reason `TableEngine` does not run OCR: a second way to load a detector is a second thing to keep correct. |

Every verified row now names its module, its contract, its fixture or an explicit "no artifact", the decision governing it, and **whether it makes an accuracy claim**. That last field is not boilerplate: every oracle here pins preprocessing and postprocessing, none asserts what a model detects. `tests/documentation_links.rs` enforces both properties - every `src`, `docs`, and fixture reference must resolve, and every model-backed row must state an accuracy position. Writing it immediately found **two classic rows, `M2-DET-001` and `M2-REC-001`, with no disclaimer**, whose talk of bit-identical tensors could be read as a quality claim. | 2026-08-05 | `MOD-003` done: resolution, and offline as an absent capability | `src/resolve.rs` turns a manifest plus a directory into three verified paths. Three decisions worth naming. **Offline is structural, not a mode**: there is no flag to set, because a manifest's `url` fields are provenance and this module never reads them. A flag can be set wrong, and an offline mode one boolean away from a fetch is a network dependency waiting for a default to change; a test asserts the property directly by resolving a manifest whose URLs are nonsense. **Every mismatch is reported, not the first** - a caller who provisioned the wrong family has all three files wrong, and learning that in one run rather than three is the point. And **size is checked before digest**, so a wrong build costs a `stat` rather than a gigabyte read, while a same-length different-bytes file still reaches the hash. Mismatch detail lives in a returned type rather than in `Error`, because `docs/THREAT_MODEL.md` keeps caller-controlled paths out of typed errors that may reach a log - so the caller decides whether to display them. Two assertions beyond "it does not panic": refusal work is timed, so a refusal that ever became proportional to a **declared** dimension rather than to the bytes supplied would show up as a hang; and refusals are asserted **deterministic**. Writing it corrected two wrong expectations I held about this project's own code - `Polygon` refuses three identical or collinear points on a **signed-area** test, not only on vertex count, and an oversized stream returns `ResourceLimit` with the limit and the actual, not `InvalidInput`. | 2026-08-05 | `STRUCT-E2E-001` first slice; a fuzz-found panic, and a claim corrected | The fuzz driver was extended to the structured modules - reading order, table composition, the Markdown formatters, and the JSON writers - because they take **caller-supplied numbers and strings** rather than a decoded image, which is a different shape of risk: `reading_order` sizes a histogram from box coordinates. It **found a panic on its first run**. Python's `a[5:2]` is an empty slice; Rust's `a[5..2]` panics, and a box whose right edge is left of its left edge produces exactly that range. The module had been committed one change earlier with that defect. Fixing it exposed a second, larger error - **in a claim rather than in code**. The previous entry described a permutation guard as though the permutation property were an invariant of the algorithm. It is not: an inverted box is **dropped from the ordering**, and upstream drops it too - checked against the pinned implementation, which returns `[1]` for the same input, not assumed. The guarantee is now stated exactly: every emitted index was supplied and no index is emitted twice, **always**; every supplied index is emitted, **only for well-formed boxes**. Duplication is the one that must never happen, because a duplicated index duplicates content in a reconstructed document. |
| 2026-08-05 | `RECON-001` first slice: the Markdown formatters | The per-label formatters `PP-StructureV3` rebuilds documents with are ported and matched. They are pure string functions, so no model, image, or artifact was needed. Three behaviours worth naming. **Heading level comes from dots, not nesting**: `format_title` strips trailing periods and counts what is left, so `A.B.C lettered` - which the numbering pattern does not recognise and leaves untouched - becomes a **third-level** heading anyway. **A level-`1` title emits two hashes**, because the format string is `#{'#' * level}`; the name and the output disagree by one, which matters when comparing documents with upstream. And **`simplify_table` prepends a newline, as does its caller**, so every reconstructed table is preceded by a blank line - reproduced rather than tidied, and the capture shows both newlines. The numbering pattern is a **hand-written matcher**: this project has two dependencies and adding a regex crate to parse four alternatives would be a poor trade, so the sixteen captured title cases are what check it. The image, chart, formula, and seal formatters are deliberately absent - they depend on P8 modules with no published ONNX export, so porting them would produce code with nothing to check it against. |
| 2026-08-05 | `STRUCT-001` first slice: the XY-cut reading order | `xycut_enhanced` is `1,830` lines, and almost all of it is per-label heuristics. Underneath sit **four pure functions** that are the algorithm itself - projection, gap splitting, and the two recursive cuts - and they take integer boxes and return an ordering, so they were capturable and matchable exactly. Every captured case reproduces. The finding worth naming: **the two cut orders are not the same reading order**. On a two-column page `yx` gives row-major `[0,1,2,3]` and `xy` gives column-major `[0,2,1,3]`, which is the whole difference between reading a page across and reading it down - so both are captured rather than one being assumed to be "the" reading order. A permutation test guards every case, because an ordering that drops or duplicates a block is worse than a wrong one: downstream would silently lose content. Two bounds added that upstream does not have: the histogram is allocated from caller-supplied coordinates, so an absurd extent is refused, and an empty box list is refused rather than allocating a zero-length histogram. |

A **separate** schema rather than a widened `ocr-result/v1`: a table result has no `lines` array and its coordinates describe a crop, so widening would give consumers a document where half the fields are `null` on any input, with no way to tell "this producer does not do tables" from "this page had none". Structure tokens travel alongside the HTML rather than only inside it, because they are what the model produced - the HTML is an assembly step over them and the OCR text. Cell NMS uses **IoU**, so it does **not** suppress containment: the captured case keeps both boxes at an IoU of `0.01` where the matcher scores the same pair `1.0` in one direction - two overlap measures with opposite behaviour on the same geometry, in the same pipeline. And region cropping **discards rather than clips**: a box crossing any edge is dropped entirely, a box exactly on the boundary is kept. The route itself has no `else` upstream - a third label raises `UnboundLocalError` - and this port returns `None` instead. Box geometry, cell sorting, row alignment, cell-to-OCR matching, and HTML assembly all reproduce the pinned PaddleX behaviour, the HTML byte for byte. Two findings. The matcher's score is **not IoU**: it is `intersection / area(second box)`, which is **not symmetric** - a cell containing a small OCR box scores `1.0` one way and `0.01` the other, so substituting IoU would match almost nothing. And `match_table_and_ocr` has two branches for its flag lists disagreeing that **nothing can reach**, because its only caller passes the same list twice; handed different lists it raises `KeyError`. That is recorded in the fixture, and this port takes a single list so the branches are structurally impossible rather than a reproduced crash. |

Three of its facts live in the **registration functions** rather than the operators, and reading either alone gives the wrong answer: `build_padding` passes `pad_value=0` where `Pad`'s own default is `127.5`, `build_normalize` accepts the config's `scale` and never forwards it, and the pad runs **after** the normalize - so the border is a normalized zero, not a black pixel, which would be `-2.117` in the first channel. This model also reads **BGR**, a fourth channel order, where layout, cells, and classification all read `RGB`. Its vocabulary is **built, not read**: `TableLabelDecode` mutates the config's 47-entry dict into a 50-entry one, so indexing the config directly would emit the wrong token for every cell. | 2026-08-05 | `TBLCELL-001` done, and it corrected `LAY-001` | Both table cell detectors are frozen, implemented, and matched. The result that matters is not the module: **capturing it exposed two bugs in `LAY-001`, which was already marked done**. The layout implementation divided by `255` where upstream multiplies by a folded `f32(1/255)` - `1/255` is not representable in binary, so the two disagree on `126` of the `256` possible byte values, meaning roughly half of every layout tensor element was one step off. And it fed `BGR` where `ObjectDetection` builds `ReadImage(format="RGB")`, swapping two of the three planes outright. Neither raises; both produce a plausible tensor. `LAY-001`'s own oracle certified the wrong implementation because **both sides made the same mistake** - its capture transcribed the upstream operators rather than executing them. `tools/capture_layout_oracle.py` is now committed and imports the pinned operators, and the fixture is re-captured at schema v2: two of three cases reproduce bit-identically, the third being the known cubic divergence. The shared path moved to `src/paddlex_detection.rs` so the open cubic defect gets fixed once rather than twice, and the resize probe is parameterized by its reference file, which produced a second measurement: `10` differing bytes of `1,228,800` at `640`, and `0` in three of five cases. |

The finding worth carrying forward is that **the two baselines implement the same-named operator differently**. `ResizeByShort` uses Python's `round` here - half to even - against the C++ `std::round`'s half away from zero, and a `512x1025` page scales by exactly `0.5` so its height lands on `512.5`: `512` under PaddleX, `513` under the C++ baseline, and every tensor value after that differs. Both directions are captured, because a case that only rounded down would also have passed with truncation. `Normalize` diverges too, folding `alpha = scale/std` into a constant rather than dividing last, which is the same algebra and not the same `f32`. Reusing `document_orientation`'s helpers would have been wrong in the last bit rather than visibly broken. The capture also changes method: it **imports and executes** the pinned PaddleX operators instead of transcribing them, so a transcription error - the source of three recorded bugs in this project - is not among the ways this oracle can be wrong. The model is never run; synthetic scores pin `Topk` exactly as well and need no session. |
| 2026-08-05 | `LAY-001` cubic divergence narrowed to `23`; two causes ruled out | Three more variants measured, bringing the total to seven against the `297x421` to `800x800` case. The divergence improved from `24` to **`23`** bytes of `1,920,000`, and more usefully **two hypotheses are now ruled out rather than merely untried**. Fixed point is not the path this dispatch takes: three variants — descaling between passes, a single descale by `22`, and the fixed-horizontal/float-vertical hybrid that `VResizeCubicVec_32s8u`'s `1.f/(SCALE*SCALE)` line points to — all landed around `74,000`, which is a different algorithm rather than a near miss. And the two-pass structure **is** right: `HResizeCubic` and `VResizeCubic` are separate structs with a buffer between them, float addition is not associative, and splitting the passes improved every coefficient form it was paired with. A diagnostic printing the differing positions is what redirected the search: all `24` were scattered through the interior, each off by exactly one, which ruled out border handling and pointed at accumulation order. One oddity is recorded rather than explained away — the coefficient form that measures better is `crop.rs`'s **warp** table rather than `resize`'s own `interpolateCubic`, by `8` bytes consistently in both structures, which is the opposite of what the source predicts and means the remaining cause is somewhere none of the seven attempts reached. |
| 2026-08-05 | `LAY-001` cubic divergence investigated, defect left open | Four hypotheses tried and measured against the only case size that exposes the defect, `297x421` to `800x800`: the current `crop.rs` weight form with float accumulation at **24** differing bytes of `1,920,000`; `interpolateCubic`'s own coefficient order at `33`; fixed point descaling between passes at `82,990`; and fixed point with a single descale by `22` at `74,043`. The last two were read from OpenCV's `resize.cpp` rather than guessed — `resize.cpp` was fetched for exactly that reason, the same way `contours.cpp` was fetched when the contour port stalled at `5/18` — and they were still far worse, which says the pass structure being reproduced is not the one this dispatch takes. The *correct* `interpolateCubic` order scoring **worse** than the warp's form is the other signal: both coefficient variants are within noise of each other, so the remaining cause is somewhere neither hypothesis touched. The best measured implementation stands, the defect stays open and named, and a page-scale test bounds it at `24` so it cannot grow unnoticed. Four measured failures recorded is worth more than a fifth guess shipped. |
| 2026-08-05 | `LAY-001` implemented, with one open defect recorded | Two findings, and the second is a correction to the previous entry. **A fifth trap**: `ToBatch` reverses `img_size` and `scale_factors` again, after `Resize` already computed them, so the model receives `[h, w]` and `[h_scale, w_scale]`. A reading did not catch it — a capture did, because the unreversed factor produced boxes reaching `y = 1021` on a `720`-tall page. Coordinates outside the source image are the cheapest available signal that a transform is wrong, and the oracle now asserts containment for that reason alone. **And a correction**: the previous entry said the cubic resize reproduces its captured cases byte for byte. True of the five committed cases, **false at page scale** — a `297x421` to `800x800` resize differs from OpenCV in `24` bytes of `1,920,000`, each off by one, because OpenCV's 8-bit cubic path is fixed point. Two attempts at that arithmetic were *worse* than the float accumulator, `82,990` and `73,910` mismatching bytes, which meant the pass structure was being guessed rather than read — so the float version stands with the divergence measured, the fixture marks the affected case `reproduced_exactly: false`, and the test bounds it at one 8-bit step and two differing samples so it cannot grow unnoticed. The corpus is the real lesson: five cases totalling about `2,600` pixels cannot exhibit a one-in-eighty-thousand defect, and a suite that passes says nothing about the sizes it never tried. `312` tests. |
| 2026-08-05 | `LAY-001` cubic resize implemented and matched | The first operator the layout path needs, verified on its own so that when the path is built its resize is not one of the things that could be wrong. All five captured `cv2.resize` cases reproduce **byte for byte** on the first run — pure upscale, pure downscale, mixed axes, a `4x` upscale, and a heavy downscale. Two details carried it. The mapping is centre aligned, `src = (dst + 0.5) * scale - 0.5`, and dropping that half pixel makes a `2x` upscale sample at integers and reproduce the source exactly in every other column — an image that looks plausible and matches nothing. And the cubic weights are the **same construction** `crop.rs` already pins against `72` captured OpenCV cases, shared rather than rewritten, which is why the first run matched rather than the third. Neither existing path was reusable and the reasons are worth keeping: `resize.rs` reproduces `INTER_LINEAR` with fixed-point weights, a different kernel entirely, and `crop.rs`'s cubic sampler is a projective warp for an arbitrary quadrilateral rather than the separable axis-aligned scale `cv2.resize` performs. The module is deliberately unreachable and says so: `LAY-001` has no implementation yet, and an operator verified in isolation is worth more than one wired into a path that cannot be tested. `306` tests. |
| 2026-08-05 | `LAY-001` contract frozen from the PaddleX baseline | The first capability frozen from the second baseline, and it justified the pin within an hour. Three of the four significant preprocessing values are **not in the artifact's config**, and two of them mean the opposite of what the config appears to say. `interp: 2` maps to **BICUBIC** in PaddleX's own table — every resize this project has ported so far is linear, so the obvious reuse would have fed the model a plausible wrong image with no error anywhere. And `norm_type: none` does not disable normalization: `build_normalize` rewrites `"none"` to `"mean_std"`, which makes the branch that would zero the mean and deviation unreachable, while `is_scale` defaults to true — so the effective transform is `x/255`, and anyone trusting the word `none` would hand the model raw `0..255` values. Two more are recorded because they are invisible today and will not stay that way: `target_size` is reversed before use, which is unobservable at `800x800` and will silently transpose the first non-square model, and `keep_ratio: false` distorts rather than letterboxes, so the per-axis `scale_factors` are the only route back to page coordinates. This is exactly the class of finding that the pinned-source method exists to produce, and exactly what freezing from configs alone would have missed — which is the argument `D-013` was decided on, now demonstrated rather than predicted. |

The output shape equals the input shape in all three, which turns the contract's "there is no resize" from an absent registration into a measurement. Text detected on an unwarped page therefore has coordinates in an image the caller never supplied. Upstream does exactly this, but an implementation must say so rather than returning coordinates that look like they belong to the input — which makes it a public API question for `DOCPIPE-001`. | 2026-08-04 | `DOCORI-001` page rotation resampling; item closed | The last piece was the resampling, and the first thing it produced was the reason the obvious shortcut fails. `src/crop.rs` has cubic sampling verified against `72` captured OpenCV cases, and it is **not** reusable: it replicates the source border because `get_rotate_crop_image` asks for `BORDER_REPLICATE`, while `RotateImage` uses `warpAffine`'s default `BORDER_CONSTANT` at zero. For a page rotation that is not a marginal difference — the expanded canvas samples outside the source at every corner, which is precisely where the border rule decides the answer, so reusing it would smear edge pixels outward where black belongs. The cubic *weights* are shared, since `crop.rs` already preserves OpenCV's exact evaluation order, and sharing them is what keeps one verified construction rather than two. `rotate_page` reproduces all `12` captured cases — three sizes including odd `9x7` and `23x11`, at each of the four angles — **byte for byte**, compared whole rather than by digest so a mismatch names the pixel. The odd sizes are the point: they are where the output-size truncation and the half-pixel centre offset both bite. With that, `DOCORI-001` closes. What is left is integration rather than implementation — where a rotation belongs in a pipeline and how polygons map back through the inverse — and that is `DOCPIPE-001`'s subject, not something to bolt onto the classic path. `288` tests. |
| 2026-08-04 | `DOCORI-001` page-rotation geometry and a one-pixel finding | Implementing the rotation started from an assumption worth checking: that at right angles upstream's `warpAffine` and a plain transpose-and-flip agree, since pixel centres map onto pixel centres. **They do not.** `RotateImage` builds its matrix about `(w/2, h/2)`, which is not the centre of the pixel grid — that is `((w-1)/2, (h-1)/2)` — so the rotation carries a half-pixel offset per axis. Measured on the `1280x720` benchmark page at `180` degrees, upstream's output equals `cv2.rotate(ROTATE_180)` **shifted by exactly one pixel in both axes**: `0` mismatching pixels at that shift against `15,795` at none. The matrix is `x' = -x + 1280` where an exact rotation gives `1279 - x`. So the obvious implementation — exact, lossless, and what any reviewer would expect — would displace **every coordinate on the page by one pixel** against upstream. `DocumentRotation` reproduces upstream's matrix rather than the tidy one, computes the expanded size with upstream's truncation, and provides the inverse map that returns a coordinate to the caller's image; a test asserts that matching the exact rotation at the corner would be the bug, and the round trip is checked at all four angles. Remaining for the item: resampling the page through that matrix with `INTER_CUBIC`. The cubic machinery exists in `src/crop.rs` against `72` captured OpenCV cases, but it is written for a projective crop and has never been driven by an affine page rotation or compared against a capture of one. `287` tests. |

A bit-identical match against all eight captured tensors is the only evidence that both are right, and a test pins the one case where rounding and truncation actually differ, since the obvious sizes agree either way. The model identifies **all four right angles** of the benchmark page above `0.92`, which a model answering `0` for everything could not, and which a rotation implementation wrong in one direction would show up as a swapped pair. That is luck, not design, and it expires at the first release. Nine types are now `#[non_exhaustive]`, following the precedent `Script` had already set.
